| 42–50s | Full width, the car still moving. VENUS lifts the sunglasses off JEAN's face and, still holding them, turns his chin toward her with two fingers; his head stops where she leaves it. His eyes are open, pale and dusted. The wind moves his hair, and they do not blink. [Fallback, A16: the same action with the car parked at the motel.] | Tyres and engine. No laugh. |  |  |
| 50–56s | In the first second she hooks one open arm of the sunglasses into her neckline. At 52 s she raises the phone and the picture narrows back to the upright frame, with the timer showing again from that moment. JEAN's open eyes sit centred under the video light, his face still where she set it. The frame shakes as she laughs. Her thumb goes to the flip-camera icon, rests there, then slides back. [Fallback: the front camera for under a second, her face lit by the video light beside his open eyes, which look past the lens; then it flips back to him.] | The wind comes back on the mic. Her laugh, softer and close. |  | REC 01:04:55 |
| 56–60s | Full width, last light. The two-seater stands in the carport of an abandoned motel under a peeling MOTEL sign on steel legs, JEAN's face still set toward the windscreen, where she turned it. VENUS leans across his knees, opens the glovebox, takes out a pistol and slides it into her back pocket. [Fallback: the pistol already in her back pocket, no hand on it.] | The cooling engine pinging, the glovebox latch dropping open, wind in dry palms. |  |  |
| 60–64s | Full width: VENUS walks the gutted motel frontage alone, the pistol grip showing from her back pocket. She tugs the padlock on the office door and looks down at the unbroken dust on its step. | Gravel under her boots, a loose panel on the sign ticking in the wind, the padlock knocking on its hasp. |  |  |
| 64–72s | At the car, VENUS lifts out his black bag, her own bag and two paper grocery sacks. JEAN stays in the passenger seat, his face still set toward the windscreen, where she turned it at dusk. She carries the bags to a ground-floor room and shoulders its swollen door open. | The boot lid, paper sacks rustling, the door scraping open over grit. |  |  |
| 72–82s | Inside the room: one battery lantern on a wooden table, a bedroll on the floor and a row of empty water jugs by the door. Her hands set down cans, full water jugs, oranges and a palm-sized photo printer, then pull a second chair up to the table. On the wall above are dozens of long-lens photos of JEAN, alone in every one; none show the two of them together. | The lantern switch clicking, cans set down on wood, a chair leg dragging across the boards. |  |  |
| 82–88s | A held frame in which nothing moves: the lantern, the oranges, the cans, the printer, two empty chairs, the bedroll and the empty jugs by the door. Above the table is the wall of JEAN photos, none of which include her. | Only the wind outside, pushing at the door. |  |  |
| 88–90s | The same held frame of the lantern-lit table, the two empty chairs, the bedroll and the wall of JEAN photos, with the LIMERENCE title card landing over it. | Silence. |  | LIMERENCE |


### V02 · 15s: Her phone keeps recording a man who never wakes; his open eyes show why, and her thumb won't turn the camera on herself.

### Writing decisions

| Choice | Why | From |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Lucas's request for this run applies to the camera department. The whole piece is written for a 35mm film look, and the script is built so the camera department has nothing left to guess. One route texture rule changes: 'outside the phone, clean' becomes 'outside the phone, 35mm film'. Her phone's upright frame keeps its compression, fingerprint smear and UI. When she lowers the phone (V01-B06), the picture goes from phone video to film grain. The stills carry the same film look. Stock, grain, gate, lenses and grade belong to the camera department. | Where Lucas's request and the route differ, his request wins. It also sharpens the route's own rule, which sets her phone against the world as it is. | Relayed user request ('Camera department needs to be full 10s', '35mm film style'); route R2 visual language |
| The V01 REC clock runs without contradiction. The phone records without a break from 26.0 s to 52.0 s. B05 now reads REC 01:04:29. B06's first 0.8 s (19 upright frames) shows REC 01:04:37 running, and the timer is hidden once the frame widens. From 52.0 s, when the frame narrows again, B08 reads REC 01:04:55. The cut from B04 (00:53:10) to B05 is the time jump. | Before this fix the timer jumped inside one continuous take (SH09-SH16). With 26 s of recording between 26.0 s and 52.0 s, it now lands on its own number. | Package review: creative_director, film_director and commissioner notes (storyboard flag 3; camera plan SH09-SH16) |
| V02 runs on a teaser clock that is deliberately not frame-matched to V01. V02-B01 reads REC 00:06:13, because it sits 1.5 s into the take V01-B02 labels 00:06:12. V02-B04 to B06 read 01:04:55, 01:04:58 and 01:05:00, which compress the eyes and the thumb into 7 s. They do not match V01's frames. | The teaser has 15 s to carry an hour's drive and the reveal. It keeps V01's timer language without claiming V01's continuity. | Package review notes (all three reviewers); brief V02 ('rebuilt from V01 footage') |
| Jean's head stays set after V01-B07. In B07 his head 'stops where she leaves it'. B09, B11, SC02 and SC18 now say his face is 'still set toward the windscreen, where she turned it', and 'head to the door' appears only before the reveal (B01-B06, V02-B01/B02, S01). In SC18 his chin doesn't give when she pushes it toward the lens, so his eyes miss her photo. | The rigor logic in the casting bible holds the B07 set. Once his head is set, the SH21 lens keeps his eye behind the cheek line, so the eyes remain the one face shot. His set head also gives the tag photo its flaw. | Package review notes (storyboard flag 6a; casting bible JEAN rigor; camera plan SH21); taste note (hands before faces) |
| Jean's black duffel is planted. SC04 shows it under his stool through the window. In SC06 it sits packed at his feet while he says 'She left. Packed while I was at work.' He shoulders it in SC08, it lies at their feet in SC09, and it goes on the white mat beside his shoes in SC10. She zips it in SC17, and it becomes 'his black bag' in V01-B11. | V01-B11's bag now has a source. The packed bag contradicts his lie without any voiceover. | Package review notes (world bible blocker 1; directors_treatment SC02 link); taste note (restraint over explanation) |
| SC07 is now CONTINUOUS, so SC06's 'My turn' cuts on action to Jean sliding the candle. SC17 is now 'MORNING, CONTINUOUS' and opens with a 4 s silent daylight insert of the two-seater, top down, at her building's kerb. She then dresses Jean while his arms still bend. | The director no longer has to stage a time jump. The car she left outside the bar comes back on screen, and the dressing plays before rigor sets fully, which holds the casting timeline. | Package review notes (directors_treatment blockers; world bible blocker 2; casting rigor timeline) |
| D01's beat sheet is now the clean version for execs. Assumption tags, 'Tactic' labels, 'Detail:' tags, '(fictional)', deliverable cross-references and plant notes have been removed. Act breaks stay marked. The crew annex below keeps every production note. | The exec decides at D01 and should be reading a story. The crew still gets every tag. | Package review note (film_director); brief audience |
| Crew annex, SC01-SC09. SC01 is V01 0-56 s. Its detail is the stop sign nobody else will reach. Her tactics are to film him close, fix his collar at the stop, and move her thumb to the flip icon. After B07 his head stays where she set it (rigor). SC02 is V01 56-72 s. Its detail is the unbroken dust on the office step, and the pistol comes from the glovebox (the SC04 chain). SC03 is V01 72-90 s. The printer and the second chair are plants for SC18. SC04's detail is the gun traded for the tin. The incident report and every case are fictional. The pistol stays in the glovebox until SC02, and the duffel is the plant for B11. SC05's detail is the first straw in the wet ring, and the inciting incident lands at about 3:00. SC06's detail is his denial of something nobody asked, while the packed duffel sits at his feet (A02, A04). SC07's detail is the chimney straw in the wax. The white-sheets insert runs at real speed, with no nudity. In SC08, the roof straw pulled is the S03 frame. The chimney is the last straw (the A18 chain), and the rideshare is unbranded (A10). SC09's detail is the dash cam, the one camera she doesn't hold. | Everything the crew needs, kept out of the exec text. | Previous internal beat sheet; package review note (keep the internal beat sheet for the crew) |
| Crew annex, SC10-SC18. SC10's detail is his shoes and duffel on the white mat. The dried drops in SC11 belong to the previous man (A13) and are never explained on screen. In SC12 the team shirt carries no logo (A10), and the detail is the straw standing in her ice. SC13's detail is the wet mark on the white table; the tin's contents stay out of frame. SC14's detail is the drops at the threshold, where she never looks. SC15's detail is weeks of full compartments, with no drug name or diagnosis on screen. Her tactics are to fold his clothes, open the cabinet and turn up the music. The breath catch is her climactic choice. SC16's detail is the four-bar loop. Her tactics are to wait, lift his arm and speak the line. In SC17 the car insert answers SC08, the sunglasses are the plant for V01-B07, and the detail is the pink cloth folded square. SC18's detail is the printer's whir. His chin doesn't give (rigor), and the prints of the next man are fictional. | Everything the crew needs, kept out of the exec text. | Previous internal beat sheet; must avoid (the tin's contents; meds as the explanation; real cases; brands) |
| D01 gains a one-page season engine (ENGINE) after SC18. It states the season question in one sentence. It shows the weekly repeat (a new man, a new round of decorum, a new wall, and the one photo afterwards) and the escalation toward him: each house stands longer, the midseason truth-teller, the front camera turned on a living man, and the tin in the finale. | The exec decides at D01, and until now the season question lived only in the internal decisions. | Package review note (film_director); brief D01 label (season question, A17); route R2 season extension |
| The pilot runs as a cold open, three acts and a tag across 18 scenes, about 30 minutes, followed by the engine page. The inciting incident ('Have we met before?') lands at about 3:00. Act One ends with the last straw going into her pocket, Act Two with the tin opening, and the tag with the first photo of them together. | This meets the pilot shape. Venus's own choices resolve the climax twice: she opens the tin after his line (SC13), and she turns the music up over his catching breath (SC15). | Craft brief (pilot); A01; A05; A06; A18; story seed |
| The pilot runs on one continuous timeline. The bar (SC04) is Thursday night, SC05-SC17 run on into the next day, the cold open is that evening, and the tag follows straight on from it. The pistol goes into the glovebox outside the bar (SC04) and comes out at the motel (SC02, V01 56-60 s). | The prints going back months already show how long she has watched him, and the gun is accounted for from the bar all the way to her back pocket. | Verified fact (gun in her back pocket at the motel); premise |
| V01 is SC01-SC03 in the order of the R2 execution example. The route's 56-64 s beat is split into the glovebox pistol (56-60 s) and the frontage check (60-64 s). B07 holds only the lift, the chin turn and the eyes. The printer, second chair, bedroll and empty jugs appear in B12, B13 and S02. | The make-or-break eyes shot (A16) carries no extra hand action, and the room reads as the one she has kept. | Route R2 execution example; A14; A16 |
| The sunglasses carry continuity. She puts them on him in SC17, lifts them off in V01-B07, hooks them into her neckline in B08, and sets them down by the oranges in SC18. | The object passes from him to her, and his face is bare for the tag photo. | Craft brief (plant an object that comes back) |
| V01 and V02 have no spoken lines, so every vo field is empty. Her laugh, written in the sound field, is the only voice. The REC timer and LIMERENCE are the only on-screen text, and finishing adds both. | V01 is specified with 0 spoken lines and V02 must read with the sound off. Generated footage can't be trusted to render legible digits. | A15; brief V01 and V02 labels; route R2 visual language |
| The straw house is laid flat on the counter as four walls and a pitched roof. Jean's own straw stands upright in the candle wax as a chimney and is the last one standing. It stands again in her ice (SC12) and goes flat on the white table before the tin opens (SC13). | Production design can build the house without asking, and the verdict reads with the sound off. The tin opens only after his line (A18). | Lucas's straw game; A04; A18; brief S03 |
| R2's hesitation is tested while Jean is alive. Her fingertip rests on the chimney straw and slides away (SC07). She takes his phone when he tries to frame the two of them and photographs him alone (SC08). | This plants SC18's front camera and sets up the season question. | Route R2 central idea; A17 |
| Jean's lie at decorum is a new line: 'She left. Packed while I was at work. I never once raised my voice.' It is answered with 'That's a no?' / 'That's a drink.' | Decorum reads as a verdict with no voiceover. He denies something nobody asked him. | A02; A04; taste notes (restraint over explanation; dry over sentimental) |
| The trance-soul track plays only from her speaker, in SC15-SC16, and she switches it off in SC16. Every other scene carries its own sound. | The music stays inside the scene, and sound design leads everywhere else. | A08; tone (music); taste note (sound design before music) |
| In the white apartment the horror is how clean it is. There are dried drops only, on the toilet rim and at the threshold. The tin's contents stay out of frame, and the finished room is held longer than is comfortable. The only sex is a three-second real-speed insert of a hand on a black shirt (SC07). There is none in V01, V02 or the stills, and no slow motion anywhere. | Tone (clean) and the must-avoid list, with ref 05's calm next to horror as the brief describes it. | Tone; must avoid (gore; the tin's contents; explicit sex; slow motion) |
| Brands: the rideshare is unbranded, the T-shirt has no logo and the 'Cracker Jack' line is dropped. 'Naked and Famous' stays as a cocktail name, pending the producer's check. | Nothing has been cleared yet. | A10; must avoid (real brands) |
| The next man's photos are two long-lens prints of a fictional man on courthouse steps. She pins them up before the printer runs, and the photo of her and Jean goes up between the two walls. | The last scene shows the situation the series repeats, with no text on screen. | A06; craft brief (pilot) |
| Taste notes followed: hands before faces (her face first at 34 s, Jean's only at 42 s); restraint over explanation; sound design before music; dry over sentimental; one detail per scene (listed in the crew annex). Broken on purpose: S01 shows her laughing face, because a poster needs its lead. The fallbacks for V01-B04 and B08 show part of her face for under a second, as the route accepts. 'The product is a tool, then a hero' doesn't apply, because there is no product. | The notes ask for a flag wherever one is broken. | Taste notes; route R2 notes |
| S01 carries LIMERENCE only. S02 and S03 carry no text. | The brief specifies this for each still. | Brief deliverables S01-S03 |


## Cast

### VENUS

- **role in story**: Principal. A vigilante who watches unpunished men for months, then plays decorum with them over a date. She films everything and is never in her own frame. V01 objective: get JEAN to look back at her through her phone. Pilot want: one true thing about love from him, then one photograph with him. Obstacle: he is the man she is sentencing, he answers with lines, and by the cold open he is dead. Change: from never being in his frame to the first photo of them together (SC18), while the wall moves on to the next man. Age 30-38, target 34. Every face reads 30+ at the bar (alcohol, no market code given), and she is old enough to have kept a wall for months and paid for the white apartment. Defaults: I keep Lucas's height, slimness and long brown hair with highlights. I break the genre default (a glossy femme fatale in her late 20s) and the generator's stock face (symmetrical, poreless, smiling at the lens) with pores, laugh lines, one higher brow and marked working hands. She smiles at him, never at us. Heritage: Black American, recorded here and never put in a prompt. It is a casting choice, not a story need, and Lucas confirms it (see blockers). If the setting is Toronto, restate it as Black Canadian.
- **screen presence**: In one frame (in a left-hand-drive car, the casting assumption; see blockers): a woman in her mid-thirties, left hand on the wheel, right hand holding a phone out toward the man on her right. Her shoulders are loose and her eyes are on him, not the road. The read: in love, not driving. Her hands lead (taste note: hands before faces). At the stop sign her left hand crosses to his collar, brushes the dust off and straightens it. Her right thumb rests on the flip icon, then slides away. She handles things slowly, and whatever she puts down lands square: cans on the table, his shoes on the mat, the pink cloth folded before she bins it. Between actions she is still and watching. She laughs with her whole torso, so the phone shakes. At the bar she sits turned fully to him, elbows off the counter, one fingertip on the straws. She never looks down at the drops.
- **performance style**: Naturalistic, played as a love story. The laugh is the only heightened thing: real, from the belly, never a cackle. One laugh voice is locked across V01, V02 and D01: low, breaking high at the end, with no words. It syncs to her face only at 34-42 s and in the front-camera fallbacks, and it softens after the eyes (V01-B08). At rest her face stays pleasant and busy with him: no knowing glance, no half-closed stare, no lip bite (taste note: restraint over explanation). Blink: at the bar she blinks less than he does. Beside the dead JEAN she blinks normally, so in a shared frame her blink is the one difference between them. Lens rule: her eyes never go into any lens, including the under-a-second flips in the V01-B04 and V01-B08 fallbacks, where they stay on him. The first time is SC18, into her own front camera, while his eyes aim past it. D01: sync dialogue, dry and quick. She plays the flirt as genuine because she hopes he will pass (A18), and her verdicts land with no change of face ('That's a drink'). SC15: she opens the pill organiser with the same face and hands as the padlock check (V01-B10). She opens one lid, looks and closes it, with no wince, no tremor and no pause on the turned label, so the meds are seen and never played as the reason. SC16 (ref 06, as the brief describes it) is the one scene where her hands have nothing to do. She lies still, a hand to her forehead, eyes on the back of his head, and says 'J. Stay for breakfast.' at the same dry pitch as 'That's a drink', not pleading. SC17: she dresses him and cleans with the same unhurried care she gave his collar. Repeated gesture: a digit rests, then slides off. Her right thumb does it on the flip icon, and her fingertip does it on the chimney straw and on the tin's lid until his line (SC12-SC13). SC18 is the first time she presses.
- **wardrobe fit notes**: 177 cm: 7 cm shorter than JEAN, 3 cm shorter than DRIVER, 7 cm taller than BARTENDER. Long neck and arms; shoulders narrower than hips. Right-handed: the phone (PR-01) is in her right hand, and the pistol rides in her right back pocket. US women's 4 / S, tall sleeve length (US sizing assumed). Constraints: both sleeves stop above the wrist bone, and nothing covers the backs of her hands, so the right-hand scar, the thumb nick and the left index mark (if the director keeps it) stay clear. No ring on the right thumb or left index finger. While she walks (V01-B10), the right back pocket shows a compact pistol grip above the pocket line without dragging the waistband. The V01 neckline holds one open arm of the sunglasses (V01-B08 to SC18). The bar jacket pocket takes the tin and a straw with no bulge (SC08-SC09). The SC12 T-shirt covers to mid-thigh and carries no logo (A10). No nudity in any piece. In the open car her hair can be loose or tied (stylist's call), but it holds one way for all of V01 and never falls over the hand shots.
- **visual identity anchors**: [macro] Build: 177 cm, long neck and long arms, narrow shoulders over wider hips.; [macro] Natural hair dark brown #3A2417: loose 3B curls, dense, soft rounded hairline. Worn past the shoulder blades, with grown-out honey highlights #B98A55 from mid-length (joint lock, stylist). The only long hair in the cast.; [micro] Long face, narrow jaw, heavy straight brows with the right one 3 mm higher; eyes dark brown #4A2E1C.; [micro] Skin #A36A48 at the cheekbone: open pores across the nose and inner cheeks, fine laugh lines at the outer eyes, three small dark freckles on the left cheekbone.; [micro] Hands (joint lock, hand double): long fingers, prominent knuckles, three pale #C99D80 marks. Right hand: a 2 cm scar across the back between the thumb and index knuckles, and a 6 mm nick across the back of the thumb at its first joint. Left hand (a proposal; the director decides): a 1 cm glossy oval burn on the back of the index finger, just above the knuckle. No other marks.
- **fictional talent**: true

### JEAN

- **role in story**: Principal. St. Jean, called J: a serial dater three nights into drinking over a breakup. His ex's domestic-violence report went nowhere (A02; the case is fictional). Bar objective: win the game and take her home. Apartment objective: get her to the bedroom. Want: to stay the wronged man in her eyes and win the night. Obstacle: every lie costs him a straw, and she already knows the answers. Change: from alive to dead, and from trying to frame the two of them himself (SC08) to being framed with her once he can no longer look (SC18). In V01 his job is to be carried: dead weight, with the one face shot at 42 s. Age 34-42, target 38. Lines and a thickening waist show the charm is older than the looks it was built on. This breaks the category default (a sculpted late-20s leading man) and the generator's drift toward young and symmetrical, so his 'hit list' swagger reads as habit. Heritage: Haitian-American, recorded here and never put in a prompt. The reason is the name St. Jean and the family D01 can give him; since it is never prompted, it cannot show in his face. He is Black like VENUS so the killing doesn't read as a cross-racial story the pilot isn't telling. Lucas confirms this with S01 and V02 in view (see blockers).
- **screen presence**: In one frame (V01, in a left-hand-drive car): a big man in black slumped against the passenger door, cheek on the headrest, face turned partway to the door and away from her camera, so only his left jaw, ear, neck and collar show. Nothing moves but his hair, and he could be asleep. After she turns him (V01-B07), his face stays set toward the windscreen through the motel. Alive, in SC04 he sits with his shoulders rounded over the glass, elbow planted, tab slip within reach, packed duffel under the stool: a man drinking to be left alone. When she speaks he props up, turns his stool to her, pulls the candle over and keeps his eyes on hers. His hands join her game: a straw stood upright for himself, his phone held out to fit them both. In the rideshare his hand goes for her pocket. From the lobby on, his eyes go to price and surfaces (the marble, the white room) before they go to her. He checks his teeth before he deals with the blood.
- **performance style**: Three registers. At the bar (SC04-SC09): naturalistic and really lifted. In SC04 he is slack and heavy with drink. From her first line he comes up: he laughs at her lines, never at his own, his eyes stay on hers across the candle, and pushing the tab slip back unsigned is the last time he looks at it. He plays the flirt for real, so the audience wants the house to stand (Lucas: 'Jean spirits began to uplift'). The SC06 lie ('She left. Packed while I was at work. I never once raised my voice.') comes out easy, with no tell, because he believes it. SC09 is appetite, clothes on, laughing into her. From the lobby (SC10-SC15) the habit shows. His eyes reach the room before they reach her, and his lines fall into a rehearsed cadence, as if he has used them before ('Who said Rome was built in a day?', 'I ain't won a ring yet'). It peaks in the SC13 speech: a man stretching for depth and missing, who covers the stumble with 'That's for you to find out.' Sync dialogue throughout. SC15 is breathing only, recorded wild: slow and loud, one long stop, then a rasp. He is face down, so there is no lip-sync. Dead (V01, SC16-SC18, S01): absolute stillness. No blink, no swallow, no breath in the chest. His eyes are open the same amount in every shot, upper lid at the top edge of the iris, aimed past the lens and never into it. One body rule stages every dead beat (a timeline assumption, not a medical claim). In SC16 his arm is loose enough to slide off her waist, and in SC17 his arms still bend as she dresses him. From the first frame of the drive his neck is fixed and never bends again, so his head and shoulders move only together, as one block. In V01-B07 his back is free in the bucket seat, so her two fingers turn the block through the short arc from the door to the windscreen. The shoulders rotate, the collar shifts with the jaw, and the block stops where she leaves it, his eyes landing past her. In SC18 the chair back and her head on his shoulder pin the block, so the same push moves nothing and his eyes miss her photo. Silent in V01 and V02.
- **wardrobe fit notes**: 184 cm: 7 cm taller than VENUS, 4 cm taller than DRIVER, 14 cm taller than BARTENDER. Broad shoulders well wider than the hips, thick neck, waist thickening over the belt. He must plausibly lift and carry VENUS (177 cm, slim) in SC14. Right-handed: he stands the straw and holds the phone out with his right hand (SC07-SC08). US men's L, 36 waist (US sizing assumed). Constraints: one black outfit carries SC04-SC18, because she dresses him again in SC17. The black reads as one mass against white rooms and gold light, yet lets dust glint at the collar (V01-B05). With his cheek on the headrest, the collar stands clear of the jaw, and nothing covers the side of the neck facing the driver, so collar, jaw and mole read as separate shapes from 0 to 42 s. Nothing rides up or gapes while he slumps as dead weight, and the collar moves with the jaw when she turns him (V01-B07). His face takes sunglasses, with the arm on the driver's side visible. His seated head clears a small 1950s two-seater's windscreen (A11; production design confirms).
- **visual identity anchors**: [macro] Build: 184 cm, broad shoulders well wider than the hips, thick neck, waist thickening over the belt.; [macro] Natural hair black #17110E: tight 4A curls, dense; squared hairline, with the temple on the side facing the driver receding further (his left in a left-hand-drive car). Top at 12-15 cm stretched length, picked out at the crown so it moves as a mass in the wind; sides tapered short; close beard with a hard cheek line (joint lock, stylist). The A16 test must show the top shift as one mass and the crown ends flutter within the 5 s.; [micro] Short, wide face, wide square jaw, heavy low brows that almost meet; irises dark brown #2A1A12, unchanged in death. Dead eyes (V01-B07, V01-B08, V02-B04 to V02-B06, SC18): upper lid at the top edge of the iris in every shot; cornea dry and dulled with a faint grey film and no wet shine; dust on the lashes. 'Pale, lost' lives in these details, never in a lighter iris (joint lock, makeup/VFX).; [micro] Skin #6E4631 at the cheekbone: visible pores on the nose and cheeks, two faint forehead lines, crow's feet. Dead state (V01, SC16-SC18, S01): the same hex desaturated, grey-ash at the lips, lids and under the eyes, dust in the creases; never lightened by makeup, the grade or the 35mm look (joint lock, makeup).; [micro] A dark, lentil-sized mole on the side of the neck facing the driver, below the ear and above the collar: his left in a left-hand-drive car (casting assumption; mirror to his right for right-hand drive, see blockers). It is the one mark visible in the turned-head frames (0-42 s), and no other character has it.
- **fictional talent**: true

### BARTENDER

- **role in story**: Supporting, one line. Works the dive bar's Thursday late shift. Objective: close JEAN's tab and clear the counter before last call. What only he does: his tab slip sends VENUS in (SC04), and the order goes through him. Age 46-54, target 50: well past 30 for the alcohol rule, and twelve years older than JEAN. Both onlookers are men because the script calls each 'him' or 'he'. Heritage: Mexican-American, recorded here and never put in a prompt. It is a casting choice, not a story need: the two onlookers are cast apart from each other and from the leads, so no heritage is tied to one job.
- **screen presence**: In one frame: a thick-set man of about fifty behind the bar, forearms on the counter, eyes on the tab. He wants to close up. He sets the slip face-down in front of JEAN without looking at it or at him, pours without watching, and reaches for glasses the moment they are empty. Nothing he puts down lands straight. He looks at the couple only when an order is due.
- **performance style**: Understated, deadpan. At rest his eyes are on the tab, the glasses and the till. One sync line ('What can I get you guys?'), said half to the counter. No reaction to the flirt. When JEAN slides the slip back unsigned, he takes it without a look. At last call he clears glasses without a word. In SC04 and the SC08 window shots he is background only.
- **wardrobe fit notes**: 170 cm: 14 cm shorter than JEAN, 10 cm shorter than DRIVER, 7 cm shorter than VENUS. Barrel chest, shoulders and hips the same width, heavy forearms. Right-handed. US men's XL. Constraints: forearms bare to the elbow, so the hand that sets down the slip reads. No logo on him or behind him (A10).
- **visual identity anchors**: [macro] Build: 170 cm, barrel chest, shoulders and hips the same width, heavy bare forearms.; [macro] Shaved head with grey stubble #8E8882 at the sides and back; full grey-black moustache (joint lock, stylist). The only shaved head in the cast.; [micro] Round face, wide jaw softening under the chin, heavy greying brows with a pale scar splitting the outer end of the left brow; eyes dark brown #3A2618.; [micro] Skin #B07B55: deep forehead lines, crow's feet, broken capillaries across the nose.
- **fictional talent**: true

### DRIVER

- **role in story**: Featured, silent. A rideshare driver on a night shift, unbranded (A10). Objective: keep his back seat clean; the dome light is his only move. What only he does: he watches them without being part of her game, and his dash cam is the one recording of JEAN she doesn't control. Age 58-64, target 61 (under 70, so no flag). His silver crown makes him the oldest face and reads at dash-cam resolution. Heritage: white American, recorded here and never put in a prompt. It is a casting choice, for the same reason as BARTENDER's.
- **screen presence**: In one frame, from the cabin-facing dash cam: a thin, grey-haired man sitting upright at the edge of frame, both hands on the wheel, his outline against the side window as streetlight slides past. His right hand rises to the dome switch and flicks it on with one finger; he doesn't turn his head. When VENUS switches it off he leaves it off, and his eyes keep lifting to the mirror.
- **performance style**: Understated, silent. He judges with his eyes only: they lift to the mirror, hold for about two seconds, then go back to the road. No reaction face and no words. The dash-cam frame is his whole performance space.
- **wardrobe fit notes**: About 180 cm; he reads long in the seat, head a hand's width below the roof lining. He is 4 cm shorter than JEAN and 3 cm taller than VENUS, but never stands beside either. Narrow, sloping shoulders; thin arms. Right-handed: his right hand goes to the dome switch. US men's M. Constraints: his head and shoulders must read as a clear silhouette against the side window and passing streetlight at dash-cam resolution. His right sleeve stays clear of the wrist so the hand reads when it rises into the dome light. Nothing branded on him or the car (A10).
- **visual identity anchors**: [macro] Seated high and thin: about 180 cm, narrow sloping shoulders, long neck, a profile that reads against the side window.; [macro] Thinning silver hair #CFC9C0 cropped close, receding high, the crown bright under the dome light; clean-shaven (joint lock, stylist). The only silver hair in the cast.; [macro] Right hand shape: long, bony fingers and a narrow wrist, the outline that rises into the dome light to the switch.; [micro] Backs of the hands: raised veins, swollen knuckles, brown age spots on pale skin.; [micro] Long, narrow face, hollow cheeks, heavy grey brows; skin #DDBBA3 with red weathering across the cheeks and nose, and a brown age spot at the left temple.
- **fictional talent**: true

## World

### LOC-01

- **description**: Venus's car, a vintage open two-seater roadster with a 1950s shape (A11). In SC04 it is parked across from the bar at night with the black canvas top up. In the SC17 insert it sits at her building's kerb in daylight: top down, clean, passenger seat empty. In SC01-SC02 (V01-B01 to B11, V02, S01) the top is down and the car is dusted. It must not look like any real make: long bonnet, rounded wings, low cut-down doors, a plain oval grille with 6 horizontal bars, round lamps, no bonnet ornament, badge holes filled, plain body-colour hubcaps. US, 2026 ASSUMED. Left-hand drive: Venus drives on the left and Jean sits on the right. Rear plate only (ASSUMED state), blank in every shot. No mirror on the windscreen rail or on the dash. She has owned the car for years ASSUMED. The last thing she did before V01-B01 was seat Jean and put his sunglasses on him (SC17). Wear: the black steering-wheel rim is rubbed from #2E2A26 gloss to #5A4636 matte in two 8 cm arcs at 10 and 2 o'clock, where her hands go. The driver's left seat bolster is darkened to #B9A98C over 20 x 8 cm from getting in and out. Dust is tracked under PR-57: the car is clean in SC04 and in the SC17 insert. From V01-B01 a gold film covers the dash top, broken by one wiped arc at its centre, where her right wrist rests while she films him. That arc is the car's one real detail. Background: only the road and the scrub move, and there are no other cars. Must stay blank: the plate, the dash dials (tick marks only, no numerals or logo), the steering-wheel boss, the wing flanks and the keys (PR-61).
- **spatial layout**: Car 4.00 L x 1.55 W. Section heights are above the ground and locked for the car's reference model under A09. Floor pan 20 cm. Seat cushion tops 35 cm (15 cm over the pan), each 45 cm wide, backs reclined about 20° ASSUMED, seat-back tops 90 cm. Bone headrest pads, 18 cm tall, on the seat backs, tops at 108 cm. Door tops 72 cm. Dash top 88 cm. The windscreen has 30 cm of glass from the dash top to the top rail at 118 cm, with no mirror on the rail or the dash. Steering wheel 40 cm across, centre 78 cm, rim top 98 cm. Seated eye lines: Venus 110 cm, upright at the wheel, 8 cm under the rail, so she looks through the glass; Jean 103 cm, slumped with his cheek on the pad. Venus's crown is about 121 cm. Top down (SC01-SC02, SC17 insert): the rail at 118 cm is the car's highest point, and the folded top lies in its well behind the seat backs at x 110-140, stack top 98 cm, 10 cm under the pad tops. Top up (SC04): outside height 1.30 m, bow underside 1.27 m, 6 cm over her crown. Walls in plan: A driver door (her entry), B dash and windscreen, C passenger door, D seat backs and boot. Anchor A/B is the driver's end of the windscreen base. x runs along A toward D (rearward) and y toward C, in cm. Bearing 0° = toward C, clockwise (90° D, 180° A, 270° B). Cabin 115 (x) x 125 (y): windscreen base at x 0, dash top x 0-30, dash face at x 30, wheel centre at x 55, y 32, seat backs at x 105. Driver's cushion centred at y 32, passenger's at y 93. Ignition on the dash face at y 50, 74 cm up. Glovebox on the passenger dash face at y 76-106: a 30 x 14 cm drop-down lid hinged at the bottom, 70-84 cm up. She reaches it across Jean's knees (V01-B09). Boot at x 150-215, lid hinged at its front edge and not locked. In V01-B11 it holds exactly PR-05, PR-06 and the two PR-07 sacks, with nothing loose. Jean's pose P1, V01-B01 to B06: cheek on the passenger headrest pad at x 108, y 100, about 100 cm up, face at bearing 0° (toward the door). Jean's pose P2, from V01-B07 (not before B07): face set at bearing 270°, toward the windscreen, where her two fingers left it; unchanged through B08, B09 and B11 and into SC18. SC04: top up, passenger side curtain fitted, driver's side curtain off and open toward the bar; parked on the far kerb, driver side to the bar at 14 m. SC17 insert: see LOC-07. - **palette**: #D9CBB0 dominant: bone semi-aniline leather on the seats, headrest pads and door cards, satin, 30%; #A9ACAA secondary: silver-grey metallic body, doors and dash, satin; clean in SC04 and the SC17 insert, dulled under dust in V01, 24%; #111111 secondary: Jean's black shirt (stylist), 16%; #2E2A26 neutral: black bakelite steering wheel and glovebox knob, gloss, 5%; #C9A45C accent: desert dust film on the dash top and his collar, matte, 4% in V01 only (PR-57); #D7261E accent: REC dot, composited UI only, under 1%
- **product placement**: No product (R2 stretch; no product reference). The device doing the job is PR-01, in Venus's right hand at arm's length, her wrist on the dash-top centre, lens on Jean's collar. The phone UI is composited in finishing. It is an original design with no OS or maker's marks, and its clearance is pending (A10). The upright 9:16 picture is centred between #0A0A0A pillars. A #111111 top bar, 7% of frame height, holds the REC dot #D7261E (2.2% of frame width) and the timer in #F2EEE6. The timer reads as scripted: 00:00:04, 00:06:12, 00:21:40, 00:38:15, 00:52:30, 00:53:10, 01:04:29 (B05), 01:04:37 (the first 19 frames of B06 only), 01:04:55 (B08). V02 runs its own teaser clock: 00:06:13, 00:52:30, 01:04:55, 01:04:58, 01:05:00. A #111111 bottom bar, 9% of frame height, holds the flip-camera icon at bottom right, under her thumb: a #F2EEE6 outline of two arrows around a circle. The side button is never framed. Clear zone: the lower-right 15% of the frame, around the icon, holds nothing but her thumb (V01-B04 and B08, V02-B06). Swap test stand-in: the icon is there only because she won't turn the lens on herself.

### LOC-02

- **description**: SC01 (V01-B01 to B08, V02-B01 to B06, and the light for S01). US Southwest high desert, 2026 ASSUMED, within a day's drive of the city. A dead crossroads town, empty for decades ASSUMED: one block of six single-storey stucco storefronts, windows boarded and fascias sanded blank. A two-lane blacktop runs north past it through scrub flats toward low hills. Right-hand traffic, with a faded double-yellow centre line and white edge lines. No other vehicle, person, animal or wire appears in any shot; only the scrub moves in the wind. She drives through the town twice (the loop in the layout), so V01-B03's storefronts at 00:21:40 are a second pass. Wear: crazed asphalt; the stop sign's face sun-chalked from red to #B8453A with its white letters intact; a sand drift across the northbound stop line that no tyre crosses until hers (PR-58). One real detail: the stop sign nobody else will ever reach (V01-B02).
- **spatial layout**: Exterior, no walls. x runs north along the road and y east, in m. The road is 7.3 m wide (two 3.65 m lanes), and the car stays in the northbound lane. The town block is on the east side (Jean's side) at x 0-45, set back 4 m from the edge line, with fascias 4.2 m high. The crossroads is at x 120, with an east-west gravel road. The stop line is 1.2 m before the crossing, with PR-58 across it. PR-10 stands on the east shoulder 2.2 m off the edge line, the bottom of the sign 2.1 m above the road, facing south. With the car at the stop line, the sign is about 3 m ahead and to the right of Jean's head, behind him in the phone frame. Loop route ASSUMED (about 10 km in about 15 min). First pass, V01-B01 (00:00:04): north through town. V01-B02 (00:06:12): stop at the crossroads. Then east on the gravel road about 3 km, south on a graded track about 1.5 km, and west on a second gravel road back to the blacktop at x -1500, 1.5 km south of the town's south end. Then north through town again: B03's first cut (00:21:40) is this second pass, with the storefronts still on Jean's side. Then north through the crossroads without stopping, into open desert (00:38:15) and toward the hills (00:52:30). Beyond x 150: scrub flats, with hills 2-5 km to the east. The sun is low in the west, behind the driver, on every northbound pass; by V01-B05 it is blue dusk.
- **palette**: #9A8466 dominant: caliche ground and dry scrub, matte, 36%; #D8B77A secondary: low-sun sky and haze, V01-B01 to B04, 24%; #3E5670 secondary: blue dusk sky, replacing it from V01-B05, 24%; #4A4744 secondary: crazed asphalt, matte, 18%; #C8B79A neutral: bleached stucco storefronts, flat, 12%; #B8453A accent: sun-chalked stop-sign sheeting, 1%
- **product placement**: No product. The stop sign carries the scene's one detail (V01-B02). The only word in this location is 'STOP'; the storefronts, road and hills carry no text. The only background motion is the scrub, which stays lower in contrast than Jean's black collar in the same frame.

S2 once she pulls away in V01-B02 (not before B02): two tyre tracks 16 cm wide, 1.30 m apart centre to centre (ASSUMED track width), pressed to darker #A89470 with sand ridged 1 cm along their edges. S3 after the second pass, not before V01-B03: a second pair of tracks, without a stop, crossing the first at a slight angle. S3 is never framed and is recorded for continuity only. |


### LOC-03

- **description**: SC02 (V01-B09 to B11). An abandoned US Southwest roadside motel, built about 1958 and shut about 25 years ago ASSUMED (ref 08 as the brief describes it): a two-storey stucco room block with a gutted upper floor, a single-storey office, a long carport, a peeling sign on steel legs and dry palms. Venus has used room 3 for weeks (LOC-04). History in objects: the office step's unbroken dust is her proof that nobody has come (V01-B10). Her own traffic has worn one bare track, 40 cm wide, #A89878 packed against #B7A98C loose dust, running about 6 m from the carport bay to her door. Background: palm fronds and scrub move, and a loose sign panel ticks. The highway stays empty, with no vehicles or people.
- **spatial layout**: Site 60 x 35 m. Wall A is the highway frontage (south). x runs east along A and y north, in m; bearing 0° = north (toward C). The driveway enters at x 17-24 on wall A. Sign PR-12 stands on steel legs at x 8, y 3: the MOTEL box is 6.0 x 1.8 m with its top at 9 m, and the OFFICE arrow is 3.0 x 0.8 m at 5 m, pointing east. Office: 6 x 6 m at x 9-15, y 12-18, its door centred on the south face at x 12, with PR-14 in front at y 11.6-12.0. Room block: x 16-59.2, y 18-27, two storeys and 6.5 m high, with 12 ground-floor rooms 3.6 m wide (LOC-04). Each door is at the east end of its room's frontage. Her room is the third, at x 23.2-26.8, with door PR-15 at x 25.7-26.6 facing 180°. Carport: x 16-30, y 12-18, a corrugated roof at 2.6 m on steel posts at 3.6 m centres. The car noses in facing 0° at x 21.0-22.55, y 13.5-17.5, its passenger side (Jean, his face set toward the windscreen) toward her door; the boot is about 6 m from the door. V01-B09: engine off, PR-61 into her left front jeans pocket and PR-02 into her right back pocket; PR-01 is already in her right front pocket. Her walk in V01-B10 runs west from the car along the frontage to the office step, about 10 m; the padlock is within reach from the gravel in front of the step, so she never stands on it. Palms stand at x 3, 34 and 46, y 5. Open desert to the north. US, right-hand traffic.
- **palette**: #CFC3A8 dominant: bleached stucco on the room block and office, flat, 32%; #B7A98C secondary: gravel and dust forecourt, matte, 24%; #7F8FA3 secondary: last-light sky, 20%; #5B6B4A neutral: dry palm fronds and scrub, matte, 8%; #6E6A63 neutral: galvanised sign legs and carport posts, weathered, 6%; #A83A2C accent: faded red enamel OFFICE arrow, peeling, 2%
- **product placement**: No product. The only legible text here is 'MOTEL' and 'OFFICE', verbatim. No room numbers, vacancy sign, brands or graffiti. The pistol grip (PR-02, #2B2B2B matte) has to read in her back pocket. Request to the stylist: light-wash denim around #8FA3B8, which gives 5.5:1; dark denim would lose the grip. Pocket map from B09: phone outline in the right front pocket, keys in the left front, grip in the right back. Only the grip shows.

Below it, the red arrow #A83A2C reads verbatim 'OFFICE'. One loose face panel, 60 x 40 cm at the top right, ticks in the wind. ### LOC-04

- **description**: The motel room: SC03 (V01-B11 to B14, S02) and SC18. Room 3 of the 1958 block ASSUMED, shut about 25 years and stripped (no bed frame, no TV). Venus has used it on and off for about five weeks ASSUMED, counted by the nine emptied gallon jugs. It is where she builds the wall and brings each man afterwards (A14). Walls: the period motel finish, knotty-pine tongue-and-groove paneling in 15 cm vertical boards, aged to amber #7A5A3C under dull satin varnish; the pins go straight in. Outlets are two-slot, ungrounded NEMA 1-15 with brown bakelite plates, all dead; the plate on wall B at y 120, 30 cm up, is missing. The last thing before V01-B12: she left at least two days ago ASSUMED, with the bedroll laid out, the wall up and the lantern switched off on the table. Wear, placed inside the held frame toward wall C: a chalky dust film #A08D72 over the fir floorboards, except two bare tracks 45 cm wide, dull satin #5C4A38, where her feet go (from the door north to the table's east end, and from the table's west end to the head of the bedroll). Three overlapping jug rings, 16 cm across, in dull #9C8468 on the table's west end at x 122-150, y 372-398. A paler 60 x 80 cm rectangle #A88458 on wall C at x 283-343, heights 155-235, where the motel's own picture hung for decades and kept the pine from ambering. About 20 empty pin holes, 1 mm with 3 mm dark halos #4A3626, along the east edge of the photos at x 278-284, where she re-pinned the last column as the wall grew; they read in SC18's close views. Background: nothing moves, and the wind pushes at the door. One real detail: PR-25, the printer, planted for SC18.
- **spatial layout**: 3.6 W x 4.8 D x 2.6 H ASSUMED. Walls clockwise: A south (entrance), B west, C north, D east. Anchor A/B; x along A toward D and y toward C, in cm; bearing 0° = toward C. Wall A: the window at x 40-140 (sill 95, head 200) is boarded with plywood #8C7A5E, leaving a 15 cm gap at the top that shows the dark sky. Door PR-15 at x 250-340 is hinged at x 340 and swings in toward D, sweeping x 250-340, y 0-90; it sticks at 35° until shouldered. View out: the carport and the car. PR-05 and PR-06 sit under the window at x 40-140, y 20-48, outside the view toward C. Wall D: the nine emptied jugs (PR-23) in a row at x 340-355, y 102-237, just past the door's sweep. PR-19 S1 stands against wall D at x 315-360, y 280-325, facing 270°. Wall B: bedroll PR-26 at x 10-80, y 190-400, with the wall-B section of PR-20 above it at y 205-380, heights 150-234. Wall C: the main section of PR-20 at x 15-277.5, heights 95-239; the bare pine at x 280-345, with the paler rectangle, is left empty for SC18. Table PR-17 (120 x 70 x 74) at x 120-240, y 330-400, 80 cm off wall C. Chairs PR-18 and PR-19 are on the table's north side with their backs to wall C; the chair backs, at 88 cm, sit below the photos' 95 cm bottom edge. They face 180°, with seat centres at y 420, x 150 (hers) and 210 (Jean's), seats 45 wide. Tabletop before V01-B12: only PR-16 (off) at x 180, y 365, and the jug rings. Tabletop from B12: PR-16 at x 180, y 365; PR-25 at x 145, y 340, slot facing 180°, on her side, next to the person who works it; PR-24 at x 195-225, y 340-360, in front of Jean's chair; PR-21 at x 182-198, y 382-397; PR-22 at x 206-238, y 382-397; PR-07 folded flat underneath. SC18 adds PR-04 at x 200-214, y 365-370 and PR-02 at x 124-136, y 331-348. Dressing master (V01-B13, S02; placement is the DP's call): the room is dressed to read from the door end facing wall C, with the bedroll and the wall-B photos at frame left and the jug row at frame right.
- **palette**: #7A5A3C dominant: knotty-pine paneling, aged amber, dull satin varnish, on walls C, B and D around and above the photos, 30% of the S02 master; #A08D72 secondary: dust film over the fir floorboards, chalky flat, 14%; #1E2833 secondary: night-street tones in about half the prints (bar window, streets at night), gloss photo paper, 14%; #A09A90 secondary: daylight concrete and asphalt in the other half (gas-pump apron, crosswalk), gloss photo paper, 11%; #5E4632 neutral: oiled pine table and chairs, worn satin, 12%; #111111 accent: Jean's black clothes, the figure in every print, gloss, 4%
- **product placement**: No product. The jobs here belong to PR-20 (none of them together) and to PR-25 with PR-27, the pilot's payoff (A06). The printer sits on Venus's side because she works it; the oranges and, in SC18, the sunglasses sit in front of Jean, as SC18 scripts. The lantern #F2EEE6 on the #5E4632 table gives 7.6:1. The bare pine at x 280-345 on wall C, with its paler rectangle, plants the next man; it stays empty until SC18. Frame lock: V01-B13 and S02 name more than five props, which is over the craft limit but forced by the script. Lock order: PR-20, PR-16, PR-24, PR-25, PR-19; PR-21, PR-22, PR-23 and PR-26 are held by count and position only. Nothing moves in V01-B13 to B14.

### LOC-05

- **description**: The dive bar and its street: seen from the car in SC04, then SC05 to SC08 and S03. A dive bar in an unnamed US Southwest city, 2026, open about 40 years ASSUMED, in a 1950s single-storey strip of painted brick #7E4B3A with a flat stucco parapet and one fan palm in a sidewalk cut-out. The streetlights are LED cobra heads, so the only amber on the sidewalk is the bar's own window spill. Inside is a long, narrow regulars' room, going by refs 01-03 as the brief describes them: amber wall sconces, a red neon strip over the back bar, a green-glowing glass-front bottle fridge, candles in glasses on the counter, and a chalkboard menu smudged past reading. It is Thursday and near empty: three patrons in silhouette at the far end and one bartender. It is Jean's third night on the same stool, with his packed black duffel (PR-05) on the floor beneath it. Wear: the varnish is worn to raw #6B4A30 in a 40 x 25 cm patch at Jean's stool from regulars' elbows; dozens of faint dull rings #5A4030 mark the counter; the brass footrail is rubbed bright #C9A15A in front of each stool. Background: the patrons move slowly, stay lower in contrast than the straws and never cross the straw house or the candle. One real detail: PR-30 standing in candle wax.
- **spatial layout**: 6.0 W x 14.0 D x 3.2 H ASSUMED. Walls: A street front (south), B west, C back, D east. Anchor A/B; x along A toward D and y toward C, in cm. Wall A: glazed door at x 470-560, swinging in toward D. A plain front window with no lettering at x 40-440, sill 80, head 270. View out: the street, and 14 m away on the far kerb, Venus's car (LOC-01) with its driver side to the bar. Wall B: back-bar shelves at y 150-1250 (x 0-45), the bartender's aisle at x 45-100, and the counter at x 100-160, top at 107 cm, running y 200-1200. Ten stools at 90 cm centres from y 260, seats at 76 cm. Jean is on stool 2 (y 350), in the window's sightline, with PR-05 at x 175-225, y 336-364. Venus takes stool 3 (y 440). PR-31 starts at x 115, y 260. The straw caddy is at x 130, y 480. The straw house is centred at x 130, y 395, between their glasses. The red neon strip is at 210 cm, y 250-900; the fridge at y 950-1050. Wall C: restroom door at x 400-480. Wall D: booths, with sconces at 190 cm. Street: an unnamed US Southwest city ASSUMED, right-hand traffic, two lanes plus parking lanes (12 m kerb to kerb), 3 m sidewalks, LED cobra-head streetlights (4000K ASSUMED) and one fan palm at x 620. The rideshare (LOC-06) stops at the bar's kerb, x 0-500, right side to the kerb.
- **palette**: #1A120D dominant: brown-black shadow on walls and ceiling, flat, 42%; #3A2418 secondary: varnished counter, satin, 16%; #C8873A secondary: amber sconce and candle glow on brass and glass, 10%; #9E1B1B accent: red neon strip, 3%; #2F6B4F accent: green bottle-fridge glow, 3%; #EFE9DC accent: white paper straws, uncoated matte, 1%
- **product placement**: No product. Here the straws are the job, because decorum is read in straws. White straws #EFE9DC on the #3A2418 counter give 12:1, so the house reads with the sound off in S03 and through the window in SC08. S03's ring is the re-wet one (PR-32 S3). In S03 nothing but her fingertip moves within 30 cm of the house, and the chimney (PR-30 S1) may stand at the frame edge. Must stay blank: every bottle label on the back bar (blank cream #D8CFBC paper), the fridge glass and its contents, the chalkboard (smudges only), the neon (a plain line, no words), the coasters, the window and PR-05.

### LOC-06

- **description**: SC09. The rideshare's back seat, seen from a cabin-facing dash cam: a generic late-model mid-size sedan, dark grey #2F3236, with no badges and no rideshare stickers, logos or trade dress (A10). The driver's history: a clean cabin, the rear seat cloth pilled to #3A3D42 fuzz over 25 x 15 cm at both outer bolsters, and a plain black phone mount on the dash. Jean's duffel (PR-05) lies across the footwell at their feet. Background: cool LED streetlight slides across the side glass (no sodium, as in LOC-05), and nothing outside is legible. The cam cannot see the mirror's face, so the driver's watching reads as his face at the lower edge of frame, eyes lifted to the mirror. One real detail: the dash cam, a second recording of Jean that Venus doesn't control.
- **spatial layout**: Cabin 1.50 W x 2.00 L x 1.15 H ASSUMED. Walls: A is the right (kerb) side, with the right rear door at x 40-110 as their entry. B is the rear shelf and back glass. C is the left side, toward traffic; its doors stay shut. D is the windscreen and dash. Anchor A/B, the right rear corner; x along A toward D (forward) and y toward C, in cm; bearing 0° = toward C. Rear bench cushion at x 20-70, y 5-145, seat at 35 cm. Jean gets in first and slides across to y 100, behind the driver. Venus sits at y 40 by the door, so her left jacket pocket (PR-03, later PR-30) is on his side, and PR-01 is in her right chest pocket. PR-05 lies on its side in the footwell at x 72-100, y 45-95. Front seat backs at x 100. The driver sits front left at y 95-135 (US left-hand drive). Dash cam PR-36 sits at the top of the windscreen at x 172, y 55, 20 cm right of the mirror (y 75), lens facing 270° (rearward) onto the bench. Rear dome light PR-37 is in the headliner over the bench at x 55, y 75, 110 cm above the floor, within Venus's reach.
- **palette**: #0E0F12 dominant: unlit cabin, headliner and pillars, 48%; #2C2F33 secondary: charcoal cloth rear bench, matte, 20%; #111111 secondary: Jean's black shirt and duffel, 12%; #B9C3CC accent: cool LED streetlight sliding across the glass and faces, 4000K ASSUMED, 5%; #E9E4D8 accent: lit dome-light lens, one beat only, 2%
- **product placement**: No product. PR-03 is present but never framed: it stays in her left jacket pocket on Jean's side, where his hand finds it through the fabric. Must stay blank: the car's badges, the windscreen stickers, the dash cam body, the phone mount, the driver's phone screen (off) and PR-05.

### LOC-07

- **description**: Venus's building: SC10 (the lobby and the stairs up to her door) and the 4-second daylight insert that opens SC17 (the street kerb outside). A renovated 1920s downtown mid-rise in the same unnamed US Southwest city, 2026 ASSUMED, where she has lived about two years ASSUMED. At night there is no doorman, and no residents are seen or heard. Honed white marble, satin brass and a quiet stair. Wear: the marble is polished to a gloss path 90 cm wide from the street door to the stair foot (#EDEAE4 gloss within #E9E6E0 honed). The SC17 insert, about 07:00 ASSUMED: the two-seater (LOC-01) at the kerb, top down, clean, passenger seat empty, with no people and no other car in frame. One real detail: the marble curtail step at the stair foot, rounded and projecting, the step she warns him about ('Mind the step').
- **spatial layout**: Lobby 8.0 W x 6.0 D x 4.2 H; floor to floor 4.40 m ASSUMED. Walls: A street (south), B west, C north, D east. Anchor A/B; x along A toward D and y toward C, in cm; bearing 0° = toward C. Wall A: a glazed double street door in brass frames at x 300-480, swinging in. Wall B: mailbox bank PR-38, 120 x 90 cm, at y 150-270, bottom at 100 cm. Stair: a marble U-stair in the north-east corner with 120 cm flights, 25 risers of 17.6 cm (4.40 m) and 28 cm treads. Flight 1, 13 risers, rises north along wall D at x 680-800, y 120-456; its first riser is the curtail step, rounded on the open side and projecting 20 cm to x 660. The half landing is at x 560-800, y 456-600, at 229 cm. Flight 2, 12 risers, rises south at x 560-680, y 456-148, through an open well in the lobby ceiling to the floor-2 landing at x 560-800, y 20-148. Her door PR-39 is on the landing's east side, facing 270°, so she walks east into the flat (LOC-08). A satin brass handrail runs on the wall side at 90 cm. The gloss path runs from the street door to the curtail step. Sockets: US NEMA 5-15. Street frontage (SC17 insert): wall A's outside face is honed pale limestone #D6CFC0 on the ground floor, with the brass double door at x 300-480 and no address numerals, plaque, awning text or signage. A 3 m concrete sidewalk #B5B0A6 runs at y -300 to 0, with the kerb at y -300. The car stands 30 cm off the kerb at x 260-660, y -330 to -485, facing 270° (west, right-hand traffic), passenger side to the kerb.
- **palette**: #E9E6E0 dominant: honed white marble floor and treads, 38% of the lobby master; #F4F2EE secondary: white eggshell stair-hall walls, 30%; #B08D57 secondary: satin brass handrail, mailboxes and door frames, 8%; #9A9A98 neutral: grey marble veining, 6%; #111111 accent: Jean in black, 5%; #D6CFC0 secondary: honed limestone facade, SC17 insert master only, 40% of that frame
- **product placement**: No product. Must stay blank: the mailbox cards, the door, any wall directory, the facade and the car's plate in the SC17 insert. The marble is the only thing here that reads as money, and nothing names what she does.

### LOC-08

- **description**: The white apartment: entry, living room and hall to the bedroom. SC10 (from the door), SC11 (off-screen sets), SC12-SC14 and SC17. This is her lure flat (A14), on the second floor, entered walking east from the LOC-07 landing. White on white; nothing sits on any surface unless a beat needs it. Kept about two years ASSUMED and cleaned daily. Before SC10 she vacuumed the rug, leaving fresh stripes, and filled the ice bucket. She missed the three drops at the bedroom threshold (PR-45), which belong to the previous man (A13). During SC11, off screen, she hangs her jacket on PR-62, sets PR-03 on the table, stands PR-30 in her drink and takes PR-01 to the bedroom. Wear, all of it marks of cleaning: 30 cm vacuum stripes on the rug, alternating matte and sheen. After SC17 the scrubbed 30 x 30 cm patch at the threshold is flatter and whiter (#F4F3EF matte) than the satin floor (#EFEDE8). A taste note is broken on purpose: there is no dish rack and no charging cable, because the tone makes tidiness the horror (the 'clean' tone line; ref 05 as the brief describes it). One real detail: Jean's shoes lined up on the white mat, his duffel beside them. Background: the sheer curtains hang still, and the city beyond shows no legible signage.
- **spatial layout**: 7.0 W x 9.0 D x 2.8 H ASSUMED. Walls clockwise: A west (entrance), B north, C east, D south. Anchor A/B, the north-west corner; x along A toward D (southward) and y toward C (eastward), in cm; bearing 0° = toward C (east). Wall A: door PR-39 at x 60-150, hinged at x 60, swinging in toward B. PR-40 at x 65-145, y 5-55. Hook PR-62 at x 160, 170 cm up, the only object on wall A and out of the SC12-SC14 table views; below it on the floor, PR-05 at x 150-200, y 5-33. Wall B: the bathroom door (LOC-09) at y 120-200, on Jean's left as he comes in ('right on the left'). A white boucle sofa (220 x 90 x 72, seat 42) stands against wall B at x 0-90, y 380-600, facing 90° (south); Jean sits at its east end, y 540-590. PR-42 at x 130-190, y 430-540, long axis along y. PR-44 at the table's east end, x 110-190, y 555-635, facing 180° (west), so her knees are about 35 cm from Jean's. A 200 x 300 rug at x 60-260, y 380-680 runs under the sofa's front legs, the table and the armchair. Wall C: a floor-to-ceiling window at x 80-420, facing east, with sheer white curtains and the night city beyond; it is the flat's morning-sun window in SC17. The bedroom door (LOC-10) is on wall C at x 540-630, threshold strip at y 895-900. The hall runs x 480-700, y 600-900, open to the living room, with PR-45 just inside the threshold. Wall D: the kitchen run at x 640-700, y 150-450, white lacquer counter at 91 cm, with PR-59 at x 670, y 300 and PR-60 at x 650-680, y 470-500. Jean's SC14 route: from the sofa past the table's east end, south across the rug to the hall, then east to the bedroom door. Floor: white satin-lacquered oak. Sockets: US NEMA 5-15, white.
- **palette**: #F4F2EE dominant: white eggshell walls and ceiling, 40%; #EFEDE8 secondary: white satin-lacquered oak floor, 22%; #EDEAE3 secondary: white boucle sofa, armchair and rug, 20%; #F7F6F3 neutral: white gloss-lacquer coffee table and kitchen run, 8%; #111111 accent: Jean in all black, her black hole, 6%; #6E1C18 accent: dried drops, matte crust, trace
- **product placement**: No product. The job here belongs to PR-03, slid across PR-42 in SC12-SC13. Gunmetal #4A4D52 on white gloss #F7F6F3 gives 7.8:1. Its mirror path: the satin steel reflects the white ceiling at about 20% diffuse, which lifts the lid to about #6C6F74, still 4.7:1. The tin's contents are never framed. Clear zone: PR-42 carries nothing but PR-03, PR-30 and the two PR-43 tumblers, and the hook and jacket on wall A stay out of the table views. The threshold drops (#6E1C18 on #EFEDE8, 9.7:1) are 3-5 mm across, so they read only in a close view. ### LOC-10

- **description**: The white apartment's bedroom: SC14 (the threshold), SC15, SC16 and SC17. White bed, white walls, a nightstand with her phone and a small white speaker, and a white side chair. The morning after follows ref 06 as the brief describes it (Jean face-down with his mouth open, Venus awake with a hand to her forehead), but the bedding is white, not patterned, because the colour theme wins over the reference. Wear: the used set is rumpled with grey shadow in its folds. After SC17 the fresh percale reads as new by being flat and even: pressed, a shade whiter than the quilted mattress it covers, with no crease anywhere, as SC17 scripts. One real detail: the speaker's physical volume ring, which she turns instead of using the phone (SC15).
- **spatial layout**: 4.0 W x 4.2 D x 2.8 H ASSUMED. Entered walking east from the LOC-08 hall. Walls clockwise: A west (door), B north, C east, D south. Anchor A/B, the north-west corner; x along A toward D (southward) and y toward C (eastward), in cm. Wall A: the door at x 180-270, swinging in toward B. Queen bed PR-52 (152 x 203), its headboard on wall C, at x 124-276, y 217-420, mattress top at 60 cm. Venus sleeps on the wall-B (north) side and Jean on the wall-D (south) side, nearer the window. Nightstand PR-53 at x 60-110, y 365-405, top at 55 cm. On it, PR-01 sits nearer the bed (centred x 98, y 385) and PR-54 farther from it (centred x 75, y 385), so her hand reaching from the bed passes the phone to reach the speaker. Side chair PR-55 against wall D at x 330-380, y 60-110. The only window is on wall D, facing south, at y 150-330, sill 80, with a white roller blind; SC16's morning daylight enters only through it. Floor: white satin-lacquered oak. Sockets: US NEMA 5-15, white.
- **palette**: #F4F2EE dominant: white eggshell walls, 38%; #F8F7F4 secondary: white cotton percale sheets and duvet, matte, 30% (fresh set #FBFAF7 from SC17); #EFEDE8 secondary: white satin-lacquered oak floor, 12%; #111111 accent: Jean's black clothes, on the floor and then folded, 4%; #E8E6E1 neutral: white wool-wrapped speaker and white lacquer nightstand, 3%
- **product placement**: No product. The job here belongs to PR-54: the room's music is heard only from it (A08). Clear zone in SC15: the nightstand holds nothing but PR-01 and PR-54, with the phone on the bed side, so her hand passing the phone to reach the ring reads with the sound off. Must stay blank: the speaker, the phone's back and the bedding labels.

## Looks

### VENUS_L01 (VENUS)

- **garment silhouette**: Closet: mid-to-high tier, bought plain and kept for years. She spends on fit, deep pockets and boots, never on a name, and dresses in the whites of her apartment plus pale denim. Story: Thursday night, early October 2026 (assumed), SC04-SC10. The city is at about 19 °C, so the jacket is on and open and her ankles are covered. She changes off screen in SC11 and hangs the jacket on PR-62. Head to toe: a bone moleskin jacket, single-breasted, 4 buttons worn open, small point collar. It is waist length, with the hem level all round 1 cm above the top of the jeans waistband. It has two pockets: a right chest welt for the phone (PR-01) and an inside left pocket for the tin (PR-03). Sleeves are cut to mid-forearm with a 4 cm turned-back cuff. Under it is a chalk jersey shell with a high round neck, sleeveless with 7 cm shoulders, tucked in. Pale-blue high-rise bootcut jeans, full length. A brown-black belt shows below the jacket hem. Brown-black pull-on ankle boots: 4 cm stacked heel, almond toe, shaft 3 cm above the ankle bone, worn under the jeans. Ecru ankle socks and an unlined bra, both hidden. Older than the story: the jeans.
- **materials colors**: Jacket: cotton moleskin 330 g/m², brushed face, matte, holds its shape; bone #D6CCBA (approx L*82, C*10). The fronts are half-lined in cotton drill 200 g/m², natural #D8CFBE; the facings are moleskin. Buttons: bone corozo #D2C7B2, matte. Right chest welt: a 2 cm welt in the moleskin over a drill bag, both lower corners bar-tacked. Behind it the right chest is interfaced with 110 g/m² cotton fusible from the shoulder seam to 2 cm below the bag. Two 1 cm cotton twill stay tapes, natural #D8CFBE, run from the bag's top corners to the shoulder seam. Inside left pocket: a welt set across the facing and lining, with a drill bag bar-tacked at both bottom corners. Hanging loop: 5 cm of drill tape, #D8CFBE, at the inside back neck, for PR-62. A flat 80 g chain weight sits in each front's hem facing. Shell: cotton-silk single jersey 160 g/m², solid, chalk #EEE9DF (approx L*93). It is plain jersey because a fine rib would moiré and fight the 35mm grain. Jeans: rigid 100% cotton denim 12.5 oz/yd², light stonewash, pale blue #8FA3B8 (approx L*66, C*13, h 260°), as LOC-03 asked, sewn in tonal ecru thread. Back pockets are plain: no decorative stitching, patch or tab. The front scoop pockets and their bags are as in VENUS_L03. Rivets and shank button are unstamped, gunmetal #5B5D60 approx, satin. Older than the story: the right back pocket is faded to #A9B9C9 in a 12 x 9 cm outline of the pistol, and its top edge is frayed. To rebuild, sand and bleach-rub the outline, and sand the edge to white threads. It is a detail, not an identity mark, because models mirror. Belt: 3 cm bridle leather, brown-black #2E2724 (approx L*16, C*4), 5 holes, with a roller buckle in blackened steel #2F3032 approx, matte. Boots: calf leather, brown-black #2E2724, hidden elastic gore, no hardware. The toes are scuffed and re-polished, with a dark sole edge and no coloured sole. Socks: cotton, ecru #E4DED2. Bra: unlined, tan-brown #9C6A4C approx, matched to her skin. No logo, lettering or trade dress on any piece.
- **fit notes**: 177 cm, US women's 4/S, tall sleeve. Jacket: shoulder seam at the shoulder point, 10 cm ease at the chest. The fronts hang straight and open, 2 cm off the shell. The hem sits 1 cm above the waistband top and 7 cm above the back-pocket tops, so all 6 cm of PR-02's grip clears it in SC04, whether she is seated or reaching back (PR-02 S1). Right chest welt: a 9 cm horizontal opening, its top edge level with the armhole base (22 cm above the hem), centred 10 cm in from the front edge. The bag is 13.5 cm deep and 10 cm wide and ends 8.5 cm above the hem. From SC04 (as she leaves the car) to SC10, PR-01 (15 x 7.2 x 0.8 cm, about 200 g assumed) rides upright, screen-in and lens end down. Its top 1.5 cm of bare black glass and frame shows above the welt all scene (PR-01 S2). The stay tapes carry the weight to the shoulder, so the right front hangs plumb, its hem stays level with the left within 1 cm and the welt stays horizontal. Inside left pocket: welt 14 cm wide, 2 cm above the armhole base; bag 22 cm deep, ending 2 cm above the hem. PR-03 rides at the bottom. From SC08 the 20 cm PR-30 stands beside it, its top 2 cm under the welt. The phone and tin sit on opposite fronts, outside right and inside left, and never share a pocket. The jacket ends above her lap, so no sit fold crosses either pocket on the bar stool or in the rideshare, and the straw reaches SC12 uncreased. In SC09 the tin's left front faces JEAN and the phone's right front faces the door. Cuff: the edge sits 12 cm above the wrist bone, so both wrists, both hands and her marks read (the right-hand scar, the right-thumb nick, and the left-index burn if kept): the SC05 forearm touch, the SC07 chimney and the S03 fingertip. In S03 no cuff comes within 30 cm of the straw house. Shell: 4 cm ease, neckline 2 cm below the collarbone notch. Jeans: 4 cm waistband with its top 2 cm below the navel, 2 cm ease at the hip, fitted through the thigh. Bootcut from the knee to a 23 cm opening; the hem sits 1.5 cm off the floor at the heel. Back pockets are 15 x 14 cm with their tops 6 cm below the waistband top, so the grip's 6 cm ends level with the waistband top, 1 cm under the jacket hem. The belt is on its middle hole in every look. The boots add 4 cm: she stands 181 cm, about 5 cm under JEAN in his shoes.
- **accessories**: Nothing worn. Ears, neck, wrists and both hands are bare: no ring, watch or bracelet. Her hands touch the phone, his forearm, the straws and the tin (taste note: hands before faces).; Nails bare and short: 1-2 mm free edge, filed square, buffed.; Set-owned: PR-01 in the right chest welt from SC04 to SC10 (set on PR-53 off screen in SC11). PR-03, joined by PR-30 from SC08, in the inside left pocket. PR-02 in the right back pocket until the SC04 swap.; Car keys (PR-61) in the left front jeans pocket from SC04. No bag, no sunglasses.
- **hair makeup**: Skin: medium-deep, warm undertone, #A36A48 approx at the cheekbone (casting hex). No base. Pores stay open across the nose and inner cheeks. The three freckles on the left cheekbone and the laugh lines stay uncovered. No contour, highlighter or powder. Brows: heavy and straight, brushed up with clear gel; the right one sits 3 mm higher and is left that way. Eyes: brown-black kohl #2A211C approx pressed into the upper lash line only, with no wing, lower liner, mascara or lashes. Lips: bare, clear balm. Hair: dark brown #3A2417 approx, dense 3B curls with honey highlights #B98A55 approx from mid-length. Worn loose past the shoulder blades with a centre part, set wash-and-go with curl cream and diffused. The curls stay defined, never stretched or straightened. Do not lighten her skin, de-age her or smooth the texture, and the 35mm look must not do it either.
- **continuity locks**: [wide] Bone jacket open with all 4 buttons undone, hem at the waist 1 cm above the waistband, brown-black belt showing below it: bone over pale blue.; [medium, SC05-SC10] Right chest welt with 1.5 cm of black phone edge above it, on HER right; welt level, right front hanging plumb. A phone on her left means the frame is mirrored: reject it.; [medium and inserts] Cuff edge at mid-forearm, 12 cm above the wrist bone. Both wrists and hands bare.; [close-up] Centre part, curls loose. In every counter hand insert (SC05-SC08, S03) the hair sits behind both shoulders.; [close-up] Kohl on the upper lash line only, no wing. Lips bare.; [SC08-SC09 medium] Left front hangs straight, with no drag at the inside pocket and no straw tip above its welt.; [SC04 insert, reaching back] Pistol grip fully clear of the jacket hem, 6 cm above the right back pocket.

### VENUS_L02 (VENUS)

- **garment silhouette**: Closet: the one thing at home she has worn for years. Story: SC12-SC16, the apartment at about 21 °C, night into morning. Head to toe: one oversized men's athletic T-shirt, raglan sleeves to the elbow, a crew collar rib with a V-notch at centre front, hem at mid-thigh. Bare legs and bare feet; nothing else shows. The raglan line and the V-notch make it read as a gym or team shirt in a wide. Which team it is (Lucas's Raptors shirt) stays open until A10 clears it or a fictional team is designed and cleared; this is flagged to Lucas. No team colour, logo, number or lettering. Older than the story: the T-shirt.
- **materials colors**: Combed cotton single jersey 180 g/m², solid, garment-washed. Body: ecru #E8E3D8 (approx L*90, C*4). Raglan sleeves, collar rib and V-notch: oatmeal #D2C9B7 (approx L*81, C*10, h 85°), so the diagonal from underarm to collar reads in a wide. The raglan seams are twin-needle coverstitched in tonal ecru thread. Collar: 2 cm 1x1 rib with a 4 cm V-notch insert. No mesh, because it moirés. Older than the story: more than 100 washes. The collar rib ripples, the side seams twist 2 cm toward the front, and the front hem hangs 1 cm longer than the back. To rebuild, enzyme-wash it and stretch the rib by hand. Modesty garment for production: seamless, #9C6A4C approx to her skin, never visible. Rule broken on purpose: the craft rule asks for a colour apart from the set. Both tones sit 5-14 L* under the walls #F4F2EE and the sheets #F8F7F4, so she belongs to the white room, her skin and dark curls carry her read, and JEAN stays the only dark mass (verified: 'The colour theme is white'; tone: clean). Story-red check (no product): the drops #6E1C18 are approx L*24, C*43, h 34°. Ecru clears by chroma at C*4; oatmeal clears at C*10 and sits 51° away.
- **fit notes**: A men's XL on a US women's 4. The sleeves are raglan, so there is no shoulder seam: the sleeve line runs from the underarm to the collar, and the cloth drops past her shoulder point. 40 cm ease at the chest. The V-notch point sits 2 cm below the collarbone notch. The sleeve hem sits at the elbow so both forearms and hands read: SC12 with the two glasses and her fingertip on the tin lid, SC15 folding his clothes and turning the speaker ring, SC16 her hand to her forehead. Standing, the hem sits at mid-thigh. Seated or carried (SC14), it rides no higher than 15 cm above the knee. Lucas's 'no underwear' is implied, never shown. In bed (SC15-SC16) the tee stays on under the duvet.
- **accessories**: Nothing worn. Ears, neck, wrists, hands and feet bare.; Nails as VENUS_L01: bare and short.; Set-owned things she handles: the two PR-43 tumblers; PR-03, her fingertip on the lid; PR-30; in SC15, his black clothes folded square with PR-04 laid on top, and PR-54's volume ring, her hand passing PR-01 to reach it.
- **hair makeup**: Skin: medium-deep, warm undertone, #A36A48 approx. No base. Pores, freckles and laugh lines as VENUS_L01. The L01 kohl is still on, upper lash line only. Hair: dark brown #3A2417 approx, 3B curls with honey highlights #B98A55 approx, loose past the shoulder blades, centre part, never straightened. SC12-SC13: she has pushed the curls back behind both shoulders with her fingers, and the hair tech resets them there between takes. SC15, from when she lies down: the curls are flattened at the back of the head. SC16, morning: the crown is frizzed into a loose halo and the back is flattened, the kohl has softened and smudged 2 mm below both lower lash lines, and the lips are dry. No fresh makeup.
- **continuity locks**: [wide] Ecru tee to mid-thigh with oatmeal raglan sleeves, so the underarm-to-collar diagonal reads. Bare legs and feet. JEAN is the only dark mass in the room.; [medium] V-notch at centre front of the oatmeal collar rib. No logo, number, lettering or team colour anywhere, front or back.; [insert] Sleeves end at the elbow. Both hands bare.; [SC12-SC13 tin and glass inserts] Curls behind both shoulders; no hair crosses PR-03, PR-30 or the PR-43 tumblers.; [SC16 close-up] Kohl smudged below both lower lash lines. Crown frizzed, back of the head flattened.

### VENUS_L03 (VENUS)

- **garment silhouette**: Closet as VENUS_L01: the same jeans, belt and boots, with a fresh shirt. Story: the day after the bar, early October 2026 (assumed). SC17, the apartment at 21 °C: at the end of the scene she puts the bone jacket on to pocket the tin and the flat straw. V01, V02 and S01, the open car at 27 °C in the late afternoon falling to 17 °C at dusk: the jacket is off and folded in the boot, never seen. SC18, the motel at night at about 15 °C: nothing changes. Head to toe: a white man-tailored button-front shirt, point collar, 7 buttons. The top two are open and the third is fastened at the sternum. It is tucked in all round, with the sleeves rolled in two 6 cm turns. With it go the pale-blue bootcut jeans, brown-black belt, ankle boots and ecru socks from VENUS_L01. Pocket map from V01-B09: phone in the right front, keys in the left front, pistol in the right back. She wears no sunglasses of her own.
- **materials colors**: Shirt: cotton poplin 120 g/m², solid, matte, white #F2EEE6 (route palette; approx L*94, C*3). Buttons: shell-white corozo #EDE8DE. Poplin creases, so the creases are locked states. S1, all of SC17: pressed, no creases. S2, from V01-B01: two horizontal creases across the front just above the belt (the seated fold) and one diagonal crease in each inner elbow above the roll. S2 holds unchanged through V01, V02, S01 and SC18 and is never pressed out between shots. The shirt stays clean: no pink, no dust. Jeans, belt, boots and socks as VENUS_L01: pale blue #8FA3B8 with gunmetal #5B5D60 approx satin rivets; brown-black #2E2724 with the blackened-steel #2F3032 approx matte buckle; ecru #E4DED2. The pistol-worn right back pocket is older than the story.
