## Assumptions and blockers

### Development

- A01 | runtime | yes | The pilot runs about 30 minutes, as a half-hour drama | if_wrong: D01 → 3000 s (~50-min) hour-long outline with a B-story on Jean's ex and the dropped case
- A02 | story | yes | Jean is on Venus's list: his 'tough breakup' is with an ex whose domestic-violence report went nowhere | if_wrong: premise → Jean is innocent and her limerence alone chose him; the series turns on her misjudgment
- A03 | story | yes | Venus has watched Jean for months before the bar, so 'have we met before' is a test she already knows the answer to | if_wrong: premise → she picks him that night from a case file, and the motel wall is built after the kill
- A04 | story | yes | Decorum is her private verdict: she knows the true answers, and each lie costs him a straw | if_wrong: premise → the game is only flirtation, and the case file alone carries the verdict
- A05 | story | yes | Jean dies overnight in the white apartment from what is in the metal tin ('let's take a trip'), so the room stays spotless | if_wrong: premise → she uses the pistol off screen before dawn and the pilot never shows how
- A06 | story | yes | The pilot ends on the first photo of them together, pinned beside the first photos of the next man | if_wrong: premise ending → she takes the photo and burns Jean's wall; the next man arrives in episode 2
- A07 | route | yes | The route is commission: D01, V01 and V02 are pitch material for drama development execs | if_wrong: objective → festival selection; D01 → 12-min proof-of-concept short outline
- A08 | music | no | 'Trans/soul' means an original trance-soul track that Venus puts on herself, heard inside the scene | if_wrong: tone music line → licensed soul track, with the clearance cost flagged on the white-apartment beat only
- A09 | route | yes | V01, V02 and the stills are generated end to end | if_wrong: V01 → hybrid, with live-action performances on a parked-car rig composited over generated desert plates
- A10 | legal | no | Real brands in the idea (the Uber, the Raptors shirt) appear unbranded on screen. Source: Lucas's script. Scope: on-screen logos in the pilot and the pitch pieces. Owner: the producer. Status: pending, nothing cleared. | if_wrong: must_avoid → drop the brand line and add clearance to the budget
- A11 | visual | no | The car is the vintage open two-seater from refs 09 and 11, so her laughter and his stillness share one open frame | if_wrong: V01 → an older closed sedan, with the ride played through the windshield
- A12 | spec | no | Pitch video is delivered at 16:9, and V02 is a 9:16 rebuild from V01 footage | if_wrong: V01 → 2.39:1 letterbox, with V02 reframed from it
- A13 | story | yes | The dried blood behind the toilet and the red drops on the floor belong to the previous man, from a night the tin failed and she used the pistol | if_wrong: premise → the blood is hers, and the untaken meds and the blood are one secret
- A14 | story | yes | The white apartment is where she lures each man; the motel is where she builds his wall and brings him afterwards | if_wrong: premise → the white apartment is staged for the date and the motel is her only home
- A15 | production | no | V01 needs 3 locations (desert road and town, motel exterior, motel room), 2 on-camera roles and 2 consistent characters, 1 voice (her laughter, no words), 0 spoken or lip-synced lines, an estimated 15–20 generated shots, 3 hand shots (phone, pistol, groceries) and no product reference | if_wrong: V01 → if Venus must speak, she speaks from behind the phone with her face unseen, so no lip-sync is needed
- A16 | production | yes | The make-or-break shot is Jean's pale open eyes revealed while Venus keeps laughing. The first test is a 5 s shot in a moving convertible in which a still, open-eyed passenger holds consistency. | if_wrong: V01 → the reveal moves to the parked car at the motel, where a static frame holds him still
- A17 | story | yes | The season question is the one man who passes decorum | if_wrong: format_rationale → closed procedural, one man per episode
- A18 | story | yes | The tin sits closed on the table until Jean gives his line about love; opening it is her sentence, so she was hoping he would pass | if_wrong: premise → Jean opens the tin as soon as she slides it over, and his line only confirms what she had already decided

### Strategy

- Market and language: English-language drama for North American streamers and networks. This is inferred from the North American brands in Lucas's idea and is not confirmed.
- Client and money: there is no brand. The client is Lucas as creator, and LIMERENCE is the working title (development's working brand). The revenue path is a paid script commission or development deal, then a series order. Subscriber value downstream belongs to the buyer.
- Release cadence (weekly, or all episodes at once) is the buyer's call. H9 tests return from episode to episode, not a weekly schedule.
- Reach: V01 and D01 go privately from the producer or agent to a named list of execs, with no media buy. V02 and S03 go public only if the producer decides a public teaser helps the pitch.
- Viewing conditions: V01 is watched on a laptop with sound on. V02 must read muted. S03 is muted in-feed.
- Evidence: Lucas's raw idea text, including 'whose case get lost', is not used as evidence. F13 (the logline) carries 'the man the system lost' as content. Because the old F15 was dropped, the former F16-F19 are now F15-F18, and the ending proof point is now F17. F19 and F20 quote development's format_rationale and the D01 label as content only, as the reviewer directed.
- Proposition contingent on: A02 (Jean's ex's domestic-violence report went nowhere) for 'the man the system lost' in the pilot. F13 supports it for the series as a whole.
- Proposition contingent on: A18 (the tin stays closed until his line about love). It shows she hoped he would pass, which keeps her love real rather than a trap.
- Proof points are held to four. F2 is not a standalone proof point: F17's ending pays F2 off and carries 'can't make him', while F2 still does work in product_relevance, S02 and the competitor fallback. F18 restates F1's support in story terms. F3, F5, F14-F16, F19 and F20 are story mechanics or season content rather than proof of the proposition.
- Proof points leave out F4 and F7-F12 (gun, colour, meds, blood, music). They are execution, or they argue a second idea (her instability), and under must_avoid the meds must never read as the reason.
- Sample sizes are consistent: 5 development readers for H2, H5 and success criterion 4, 6 viewer calls for H1, and 5 cold viewers for H4 and H9.
- Comparable titles in H3 are common knowledge, unverified and internal only. The phrase 'female Dexter' stays out of all public-facing material.
- Success thresholds are studio go/no-go lines, not market benchmarks. No statistic is claimed anywhere.
- Taste notes, which the file flags as example notes and not Lucas's own: the proposition follows 'Restraint over explanation' (it names her want, never her code) and 'Dry over sentimental', and breaks none. 'The product is a tool, then a hero' does not apply because there is no product.
- Blocker (Lucas): Alternative: key_message ('Venus can punish any man the system lost, but her strong desire for reciprocation is the one thing she can't satisfy: no man she hunts ever loves her back.') is not carried as the proposition. The main reason: 'no man she hunts ever loves her back' contradicts A17, the season question D01 must carry (the one man who tells the truth at decorum and seems to love her back). It is also a 29-word line with two beats, and 'any man' and 'the one thing' are not supported by any fact. Proposed instead: 'Punishing the man the system lost can't make him love her back.' This leaves the season question open. → Approve the 12-word proposition, or keep key_message as pitch copy with 'any', 'the one thing' and 'ever' removed so it no longer rules out A17.
- Blocker (Producer): The competitor check (H3) has not been run. No one has checked the clause only LIMERENCE owns (she falls for every man she sentences, and the sentence is how she keeps him) against the nearest threats, You, Killing Eve and Promising Young Woman, or against current and announced slates. The comps stay internal. → Before the first send, check those three titles first, then scan current and announced slates and loglines. If one matches, sharpen the proposition to the wall with no photo of them together (F2).
- Blocker (Lucas / Producer): Permission is not established: there are no credits, attached producer or prior work that an exec could check. → Supply one checkable credential for the cover note (a produced short, an attached producer or showrunner, a festival selection), or confirm there is none.
- Blocker (Lucas): A02 is unconfirmed. Whether Jean is guilty decides whether 'the man the system lost' holds for the pilot. → Confirm A02. If Jean is innocent, the proposition becomes 'Punishing the man she chose can't make him love her back.' and H1 is retested.
- Blocker (Producer): There are no flight dates, no exec send list and no decision on releasing V02 and S03 publicly. Without them, the success windows, the rate denominator and whether social metrics apply cannot be set. → Supply send dates, the list size and a decision on public release. The client sets targets or accepts the labeled studio thresholds.

### Routes

- The three most familiar openings in this genre (internal, no titles named): (1) a vigilante killer's cold open with a voiceover laying out the code; (2) the femme-fatale seduction with slow-motion glamour and a last-scene reveal that she is the killer; (3) a true-crime pastiche with case-file cards, news audio and 'unsolved' titles. All three routes avoid 1 and 3. R1 subverts 2: her touch asks rather than controls. R2 subverts the killer's trophy photo. R3 subverts 2 by making the seduction game a chance rather than a trap.
- Ten one-liners were drafted; the three kept differ in reframe, device and structure. R1: linear, objective camera, the hand as device; its idea costs 'a killer's touch is control'. R2: told through her phone's frame, widening at the reveal, the front camera as device; it costs 'the photos are trophies'. R3: two braided timelines, omniscient POV, the straws as device; it costs 'the seduction game is a trap'. [changed] R1's previous idea ('The kill is already over; the only suspense left is whether he'll love her back') restated the development premise, so every route's example served it. It failed the pairing test and was replaced. Other cut drafts: 'the sentence is the first day he can't walk out' (reads as control, against F1) and 'a dead man can't lie at decorum'.
- There is no product. product_role describes the job of the device that carries the proposition and Venus's role in frame. The taste note 'the product is a tool, then a hero' does not apply.
- All beats are tagged G or C: V01, V02 and the stills are generated end to end (A09), no shoot budget is authorised and no renders were supplied, so no beat is tagged P or R. Every reveal beat depends on the A16 test shot.
- Creative additions not found in Lucas's idea or the facts, for Lucas to approve: the dead traffic light, the dust devil with her arm catching his weight, and the unreturned squeeze (R1); the stop sign nobody else will reach, the REC timer, the flip-camera icon, the under-a-second front-camera flip, the phone's video light, turning his chin with two fingers, and the battery lantern (R2); the bent straw in his pocket and the wind lifting it (R3); the oranges (all routes).
- [changed] R2, question for Lucas: it changes his cold open's camera. 40 of 90 s are seen inside her phone's upright frame (0-34 s and 50-56 s); her face is withheld until 34 s and Jean's until 42 s; the pilot's ending (A06) becomes her first use of the front camera for a photograph. The fallback's under-a-second flip is a live preview, not a photograph, so A06's 'first photograph of them together' still stands. No beat is dropped.
- [changed] R2's time and light hold together: the recording runs from REC 00:00:04 in low gold sun to 00:58:40 at blue dusk and 01:04:55 on his eyes. The third hand is gone: she fixes his collar only when stopped at the stop sign, and she only films or touches him one hand at a time while driving.
- [changed] R3, question for Lucas: braiding a silent bar night into V01 spends the pilot's flashback. The braid now stops with one straw still standing. The last straw is his line about love in the white apartment (F16), and the tin opens only after it (A18); both stay in D01. Lucas's bar dialogue stays in D01 because V01 has no spoken lines (A15). Decorum as a verdict rests on A04.
- V01 in every route never shows or implies how Jean died: no wound, no tin, no method. The only blood rule applies to the white apartment, which is in D01 and not in V01.
- The car is the vintage open two-seater (A11). Silver is used in R1's palette to sit against gold and black. Red (ref 11) is production design's call and does not change the routes.
- [changed] Every beat runs at real speed, and no route uses an aerial or drone transition. R1's 24-32 s is now the dust devil and her arm catching his weight; R3's 44-50 s is the wind lifting the straw from his pocket. There is no nudity or sex in V01, V02 or the stills. Nothing on screen is branded; clearance is still pending (A10).
- [changed] Rights: R2's phone UI (REC dot, running timer, flip-camera icon) is an original design with no OS or maker's marks. Source: this route. Scope: V01, V02 and S01. Owner: production design. Status: clearance pending with the producer under A10, not claimed as cleared. If it reads as a real operating system, simplify to the REC dot and timer and play the hesitation as the front-camera flip.
- [changed] R2's season extension, a proposal for Lucas that is not yet in D01: the front camera turns on only once per man, at the table, afterwards, so the season question (A17) can become the man she turns it on while he is still alive.
- Venus and Jean are fictional, and neither is modelled on a face in Lucas's references. The references are cited only as the brief describes them; no reference was inspected in this run.
- Taste notes followed in all routes: hands before faces (Venus's first face at 32 s in R1, 34 s in R2 and 58 s in R3; Jean's head stays turned to the door, showing only collar, jaw and sunglasses, until his eyes, the one face shot); sound design before music (no score in V01; the trance-soul track belongs to the white apartment in D01); restraint over explanation (no voiceover and no text beyond the title); one real detail per scene (R1 the dead traffic light, R2 the stop sign, R3 the bent straw). [changed] R2's camera-roll scroll is cut because the wall already says none of them are together (F2); 82-88 s is now a held frame. Bent on purpose: R2's 18-26 s fallback shows her lower face for under a second before 34 s. R3's braid still asks the viewer to link the straws to the drive, which leans toward explanation, but it no longer shows the last straw fall, so the bend is smaller and is accepted as the brave route's cost.
- 'Female Dexter' and the comparable titles stay internal and appear in no route or deliverable.
- [changed] Revision log for this round: R2 repeats the flip hesitation after the eyes (50-56 s) and the rationale quotes it; the no-thumb fallback is replaced by the front-camera flip; the third hand is removed (stop sign); the timer matches the light; Jean's face is held back until 42 s; the camera-roll scroll is cut; the recurring device is restated; S01 is recomposed; the phone UI's rights are named. R1's idea is replaced and both squeeze beats are tagged with fallbacks. Aerials are cut in R1 and R3. R3's braid stops with one straw standing, and its V02 opens on a straw being drawn out.
- Blocker (Lucas): The recommendation quotes the strategy's proposition ('Punishing the man the system lost can't make him love her back.'), which Lucas has not approved yet (strategy blocker 1). For the pilot it also rests on A02 (Jean's guilt). → Approve the proposition or the trimmed key_message, and confirm A02. All three routes hold either way; only the rationale's quote changes.
- Blocker (Generation supervisor): Every route's reveal depends on the untested A16 shot: a still, open-eyed passenger in a moving open car who stays consistent across shots. → Run the 5 s A16 test before building V01. If it fails, every route moves the eyes reveal to the parked car at the motel, and V02 is recut from that shot.

### Script

- 'Camera department needs to be full 10s' is read as a quality bar: the camera department's work should score 10 out of 10. It might instead mean every generated shot is rendered as a full 10 s clip. The script already allows that, because no V01 or V02 beat runs longer than 10 s, so each beat can be cut from inside one 10 s render. The camera department should confirm which reading Lucas meant.
- The 35mm film look covers the full-width picture in V01 and V02 and all three stills. The phone's upright frame stays phone video, as a deliberate contrast. If Lucas wants the phone frame on film too, the camera department can grade it, and no beat changes.
- The scene timings in the D01 headings are estimates for a roughly 30-minute half hour (A01).
- The SC17 insert shows the car's return. Fetching it happens off screen between SC16 and SC17, so 'CONTINUOUS' means the same morning, before she dresses him. Moving Jean from the apartment into the passenger seat also happens off screen, between SC17 and SC01.
- The rigor logic comes from the casting bible as the review notes relay it. It is not medically timed. The script asks only three things of it: his arm is loose in SC16, his arms still bend in SC17, and his head stays where she set it from V01-B07 on.
- Creative additions in D01 for Lucas to approve or strike: the pistol traded for the tin and the incident report (SC04); Jean's black duffel (SC04-SC10, SC17); the flat straw house and the tab slip (SC05); Jean's lie and 'That's a drink.' (SC06); the chimney straw and her fingertip on it (SC07); his attempt to photograph the two of them and her photo of him alone (SC08); the dash cam and dome light (SC09); 'Mind the step' and the shoes on the white mat (SC10); the straw in her ice and its wet mark (SC12-SC13); the pill organiser, the breath catch and her hand passing the phone (SC15); 'J. Stay for breakfast.' (SC16); the car insert, the sunglasses and the scrubbed drops (SC17); the printer, the second chair, the courthouse-steps photos and his chin not giving (SC03, SC18); and the season engine's escalation (each house standing longer, the midseason truth-teller, the tin in the finale).
- Additions in V01 beyond the route's execution example: the pistol taken from the glovebox (56-60 s), the sunglasses hooked into her neckline (50-51 s), and the printer, second chair, bedroll and empty jugs (72-90 s). She shoulders open a swollen door in place of Lucas's 'breaks into the motel'.
- In SC18 the next man's photos go up before the photograph of her and Jean. She takes the photo even though his eyes miss the lens, and that imperfect first photo is kept on purpose.
- Lucas's dialogue is kept, with light spelling and grammar cleanup. The trust/lust exchange is trimmed, not rewritten.
- 'Naked and Famous' is treated as a cocktail name, not a brand. The producer confirms this under A10.
- The phone UI (REC dot, timer, flip-camera icon) is an original design added in finishing. Its clearance is pending with the producer (A10) and is not claimed as cleared.
- The crew annex sits in decisions because the output carries a single beat sheet, and that beat sheet is now the clean version for execs.
- Jean, Venus, Jean's ex, the next man and every case are fictional. No character is modelled on a face in Lucas's references. No reference image was inspected in this run; they are cited only as the brief describes them.
- The phrase Lucas used as an internal comparison does not appear in any deliverable.
- Blocker (Director / generation lead): A16: Jean's open, unblinking eyes in a moving convertible (V01 42-56 s; V02 4-13 s) have not been tested for consistency. The glovebox reach across his knees (V01-B09) is a second shot that depends on a hand holding up. → Run the 5 s test shot. If it fails, use the written fallback and play V01-B07 with the car parked at the motel. If the glovebox reach fails, use the B09 fallback, with the pistol already in her pocket. No story beat changes.
- Blocker (Camera department / Lucas): The meaning of 'full 10s' and how far the 35mm film look extends (full-width picture and stills, but not the phone frame) need confirming against Lucas's intent. → Confirm the reading. If 'full 10s' means 10 s renders, cut each beat from inside one 10 s clip. If the phone frame should also be on film, grade it that way. The script needs no change either way.
- Blocker (Producer): Clearance for the phone UI and the brands is pending (A10). → If the UI reads as a real operating system, keep only the REC dot and timer, and play the hesitation as the under-a-second front-camera flip (the fallbacks in V01-B04, V01-B08 and V02-B06). Confirm that 'Naked and Famous' needs no clearance.
- Blocker (Lucas): The creative additions and the season engine's escalation, listed in assumptions, need Lucas's approval. → Approve or strike each item. Any of them can go without losing a turn except three: the last-straw chain (SC07-SC08, SC12-SC13), which carries A18 and the season question; the breath catch in SC15, which carries Venus's climactic choice; and the duffel, which is now the only source for V01-B11's bag.

### Cast

- Car side (new this round). The cast is locked for a left-hand-drive car, the default in the US and Canada. That fits the assumed setting and S01, which is shot from behind her right shoulder with JEAN beside the phone. JEAN sits on her right, turned partway toward his right door, so her phone sees his left side, and his mole and further-receding temple sit on his left. VENUS's one-frame read (left hand on the wheel, right hand holding the phone out toward him) holds only in this car. Her hand marks are bodily and never move. JEAN's two side marks do, so the earlier line 'the marks don't move either way' is struck for him. The mirror rule and its owner are in the blockers.
- Hand shot mapping (moved out of VENUS's anchor this round). It is taken from the camera-plan citations in the review notes; the camera plan itself is not in this prompt, so the generation lead checks it against the plan. Right thumb nick, on the flip icon: V01-B04 (V01-SH07, his turned head); V01-B08 (V01-SH17, his open eyes); V02-B06 (V02-SH07, cut from V01-SH17 footage of his open eyes, not from V01-SH07); SC18 (D01, no shot ID yet). Right-hand scar: V01-B07, the glasses lift and chin turn (V01-SH14-SH15), and V02-B03, reframed from the same footage; V01-B08, the sunglasses hook (V01-SH16); SC18, on the phone. Left index burn, if kept: V01-B02, the collar brush (V01-SH02), and V02-B01 (V02-SH01, cut from the V01-SH02 take).
- The left index mark is a proposal, and the director's call decides it, because the brief leaves the hand in each shot to the director. Two reviewers (creative director, commissioner) asked for it; the film director asked for none. The case for it: V01-B02 and V02-B01 are the first time any part of her is on screen, so 'hands before faces' needs her identity there, and at 1 cm (not 5 mm) it survives phone compression in V02's 1.5 s opener. If it is struck, those shots carry no anchor and the right-hand pair stands alone.
- JEAN's alive register is now split at the lobby. In SC05-SC08 he is really lifted, so the flirt plays for real, the audience wants the straw house to stand, and the season question keeps its doubt. The habit tells start in SC10 and peak in the SC13 speech, which Lucas marks as a BS answer. The SC06 lie stays tell-free.
- Rigor is one block rule that stages both V01-B07 and SC18. It is a timeline assumption, not a medical claim. The note starts the fixed neck at dusk; I start it at the drive's first frame, because V01-B01 to V01-B03 play in late-afternoon sun and his head must not bob or shift between cuts there either. It matches the script: a loose arm in SC16, arms that still bend in SC17 ('MORNING, CONTINUOUS'), and a set neck through the cold open. The rule needs a shallow rest pose (face only partway to the door) so a short block turn brings his face to the windscreen. The director sets the exact angle.
- JEAN's hair is settled now, not after the test (joint lock, stylist). The tight 4A texture and density stay, and the top goes to 12-15 cm stretched length, picked out at the crown, so it moves as a mass. The A16 test confirms this choice; the texture never changes after Lucas approves heritage.
- Lucas's relayed request ('Camera department needs to be full 10s', '35mm film style') belongs to the camera department, and the script already applies it: 35mm outside the phone, phone compression inside it. The casting consequences are three. Skin hex values are set under neutral light before the grade. The grade and grain must never lift JEAN's skin to signal death. The micro anchors (pores, freckles, JEAN's mole, VENUS's hand marks) are sized to read in close-ups through both grain and phone compression; wide shots rely on the macro anchors.
- The cast is exactly the script's four IDs: VENUS, JEAN, BARTENDER and DRIVER. The next man in the courthouse-steps prints (SC18, ENGINE) is not cast, because he appears in photos only. Production design supplies two long-lens prints of a fictional man who must not share JEAN's build or hair or resemble any real person, and he gets a profile when episode 2 is written. JEAN's ex (name blacked out) is never on screen. There is no voiceover character.
- Setting: the script never names a country. The '-American' heritages, the US sizes and the assumed alcohol minimum of 25 rest on a US setting, which fits the desert drive and the motel. Lucas's Raptors shirt, now an unbranded team T-shirt, points to Toronto. If the setting is Toronto, the heritages are restated as Canadian with no change to any anchor, the sizes convert, the 30+ floor stands, and the car stays left-hand drive.
- Alcohol rule: no market code was given, so the minimum is assumed to be 25 and every visible face reads 30+. Targets are 34, 38, 50 and 61, and bar background patrons in SC04-SC08 also read 30+.
- Heritage: all four are casting choices, not script requirements. None goes into an anchor or a prompt. JEAN's reason is his name. VENUS is matched to him so the killing doesn't read as a cross-racial story. BARTENDER and DRIVER are cast apart from each other and from the leads. A proposal for D01: the men she sentences vary in heritage across the season, so 'the man the system lost' is never tied to one heritage.
- Lucas's 'tall slim model straight off your latest Vogue cover' is kept as height, slimness and bearing, not a glossy finish, because the tone rejects the glossy femme fatale. His optional blonde highlights are kept as a joint lock with the stylist.
- 'Pale' on JEAN comes only from desaturation, grey-ash at the lips and lids, dust, a dry grey-filmed cornea and eyes that never blink. It never comes from a lighter skin or iris hex, and the 35mm grade never lifts it.
- Squaring objects belongs to VENUS alone, as the clean-horror tell. BARTENDER puts things down off-angle, and JEAN turns his stool to her rather than squaring up.
- No reference was inspected; I worked only from the brief's descriptions. To stay clear of them, JEAN has no locs and no arm tattoo (ref 06), VENUS is not pale or auburn (ref 04), and no one is modelled on any face in refs 01-11.
- Rights and consents are no longer left in the assumptions. The laugh voice, the hand double, the intimacy coordinator and the real-person resemblance check each have an owner in the blockers. No performer is attached, and no one's availability, likeness or voice is claimed.
- The script has no child. No one is flagged for visible difference, religious dress, pregnancy or age 70+ (DRIVER's target is 61). Mental health is flagged for human review in the blockers.
- Taste notes. 'Hands before faces': VENUS's hand marks carry her identity before her face at 34 s, and JEAN's neck mole holds him before his face at 42 s. 'Restraint over explanation': no knowing look to the lens, no menace register, and the organiser played flat. 'Dry over sentimental': her verdicts and 'J. Stay for breakfast.' land at one dry pitch. Casting breaks no note on purpose; S01's face was already accepted upstream.
- Blocker (Film director, with the generation lead): VENUS's hand anchors changed. The nick is now on the right thumb, and a 1 cm burn on the back of the left index finger is proposed. K03-HND-VENUS and the hand-double brief still carry the old left-thumb nick. The film director asked for no left-hand anchor. The shot mapping in the assumptions comes from the review notes' citations, not from the camera plan itself. → The director keeps or strikes the left index mark, and that call is final. The generation lead checks the mapping against the camera plan, then re-locks K03-HND-VENUS on the confirmed set: on the right hand, the 2 cm scar and the 6 mm thumb nick; on the left, the 1 cm burn if kept; all pale #C99D80. This happens before any hand shot renders (V01-B02, V01-B04, V01-B07, V01-B08, V02-B01, V02-B03, V02-B06, SC18). The hand double is briefed on the same set, and any render showing the left-thumb nick is discarded.
- Blocker (Production design): The car side is unconfirmed. JEAN's mole and further-receding temple are placed for a left-hand-drive car. In a right-hand-drive two-seater he sits on her left and turns toward his left door, so her phone sees his right side, and the one mark that holds him from 0 to 42 s is out of frame. → Confirm left-hand drive before any K03 render of JEAN or the car. If right-hand drive is chosen, apply the mirror rule before locking. JEAN's mole, his further-receding temple and the visible sunglasses arm move to his right. VENUS's hand marks stay, but her one-frame read changes: the phone arm crosses her body toward him, and her left becomes the near hand for the collar brush. The camera department then re-checks S01's over-the-right-shoulder composition.
- Blocker (Producer): Rights and consents have no owner yet. Source: this casting bible. Scope: (a) VENUS's laugh, the only voice in V01, V02 and D01; (b) the hand double for the hand shots listed in blocker 1; (c) if D01 is shot live action (A09 if_wrong), an intimacy coordinator and every performer's written consent for the SC07 insert, SC09 and SC14-SC16. Status: pending. Nothing is cleared, no performer is attached, and no one's availability, likeness or voice is claimed. → Generate the laugh, or record it from a consenting fictional-cast performer; no voice is cloned from a real person. Engage the hand double on a written release covering V01, V02 and SC18. Book the intimacy coordinator before any live-action D01 schedule. Record each clearance before the pitch pieces circulate.
- Blocker (Generation lead, with the producer): Generated faces can land on a recognisable real person, and S01 and V02 travel without D01's context. → Before S01 or V02 leaves the team, run a real-person resemblance check on the locked VENUS and JEAN faces, and on BARTENDER and DRIVER before any D01 material circulates. If a face reads as a real person, regenerate it within the same anchors and check again. Record the result with the producer. Status: pending.
- Blocker (Producer): No market alcohol code was given, and the bar, the drinks and the tin put every visible adult under the alcohol rule. → Confirm the target markets. Until then the cast assumes a minimum of 25, and every visible face, bar background included, reads 30+. If any market sets a higher minimum or bans showing people drinking, re-age the cast or recut the bar scenes and S03.
- Blocker (Producer, with a sensitivity reader): The script pairs a killer with medication she hasn't taken in weeks, and Lucas framed it as mental health. That is the representation risk the must-avoid list names (meds treated as the explanation for the killings), and the craft brief flags disability, which can include mental illness, for human review. → Get a human or sensitivity review of SC15 and of how D01 frames her meds before the pitch pieces circulate. Casting holds the organiser beat to the padlock-check register (no wince, no tremor, no pause on the label) and never ties her performance to a diagnosis. The reviewer confirms this or asks for changes.
- Blocker (Lucas (creative approval)): Heritage for all four characters is a casting choice, not a script requirement, and the setting is unconfirmed (US assumed; the Raptors shirt points to Toronto). The pieces that circulate raise the stakes: S01 and V02 make a dead Black man the key image beside a laughing woman, and they travel without D01's context. → Lucas approves or reassigns with S01 and V02 roughs in front of him, not from this text alone. If anything changes, redo skin hex, hair texture and JEAN's dead-state notes together, keep VENUS and JEAN matched or say why not, and restate the heritages for the confirmed country.
- Blocker (Director / generation lead, with the stylist): JEAN's dead anchors depend on the A16 test. In a moving convertible, under the 35mm look, a still, open-eyed passenger must hold his identity, his dead eyes (upper lid at the top edge of the iris, a dry grey-filmed cornea, dusted lashes), a fixed neck that never bobs, and hair that moves, because V01-B07 and V02-B04 prove death with 'the wind moves his hair'. → Run the 5 s A16 test on the locked anchors with the 35mm look applied. It passes if the top of his hair shifts as one mass and the crown ends flutter within the 5 s while his head and shoulders stay still and his eyes don't blink. The hair is already chosen (12-15 cm stretched length, picked out at the crown), so the test confirms it; if the top moves too little, the stylist picks it out further within that range and never changes the tight 4A texture. If the pallor or the dry cornea doesn't read through the grain, carry his death through dust, unblinking eyes and a desaturated grade, never a lighter hex, and use the parked-car fallback.

### World

- Lucas's request (the camera department at full quality, a 35mm film style) belongs to the camera department. Every hex here is a pre-grade value under an even key. The smallest marks (3-5 mm drops, 1 mm pin holes, the 6 mm lens smear) are sized for close views and need to hold through the film grain. Every prop state is keyed to a script beat, not a shot, so the states hold whatever shots the camera department sets.
- Shot IDs are removed from the bible (the earlier SH09, SH17-SH18, SH21 and SH23-SH24). PR-01, PR-07 and PR-16 are keyed to V01-B05, B09, B11 and B12 only, because shot numbers belong to the camera plan and will change in the 35mm rework.
- Place and year: an unnamed US Southwest city, 2026 (ASSUMED), for the bar, the rideshare and the white apartment, within a day's drive of the desert road and the motel, on the script's one continuous timeline. Locked against generator drift: right-hand traffic; left-hand-drive cars; a rear-plate-only state (ASSUMED) with every plate blank; English signage; a red octagon 'STOP'; NEMA 5-15 sockets in the 2026 apartment and lobby; two-slot NEMA 1-15 outlets in the 1958 motel room.
- Street lighting is LED cobra heads (4000K ASSUMED). The bar's window spill carries the amber on the sidewalk, and the rideshare cabin gets cool LED slides (#B9C3CC).
- All dimensions, weights, ages and tenures are ASSUMED from the category, since none are verified. These include the car, the rooms, the tin, the printer, the prints, the bag weights (duffel about 6 kg, holdall about 5 kg, sack 1 about 3 kg, sack 2 about 8.5 kg), the 1958 motel build, about five weeks at the motel, about two years in the apartment and the roughly 10 km loop in LOC-02.
- LOC-01's section is recomputed for a real period roadster and locked for the car's reference model under A09. Cushion tops 35 cm, dash top 88 cm, windscreen rail 118 cm with no mirror, headrest pad tops 108 cm. Eye lines: Venus 110 cm, 8 cm under the rail, so she looks through the glass; Jean 103 cm, slumped. Venus's crown is about 121 cm. Top-up outside height 1.30 m (bow underside 1.27 m), 6 cm over her crown. The folded top's stack top is 98 cm, under the pads. These are design values, not measurements from cast or doubles, and the earlier cushion-raise fallback is withdrawn.
- The wiped dust arc moved to the dash-top centre (rear edge, y 45-65), where her right wrist can rest while the phone points sideways at his collar. Its size, hex and cause are kept, and the lens stays on his collar and the left arm of his sunglasses until V01-B07.
- PR-01's side button moved to the left frame edge, 4 cm from the top. In the upright right-hand grip that edge sits under her index finger, which is what the earlier reviewers asked for; the right edge is under her thumb. The press is never framed: V01-B05 is seen through the phone, so the light just comes on and no hand action is generated, as this round's note prefers.
- Where the earlier reviewers conflicted: PR-04's source is PR-05's outside pocket (creative director, backed by the stylist's 'No sunglasses in SC04-SC15'), which overrules the commissioner's shirt-placket option. For SC04-SC10, PR-01 sits in the right chest pocket of VENUS_L01's jacket. From V01-B09, the right front jeans pocket overrules the storyboard's left.
- PR-01 is in her right hand for V01-B01 to B05 and B08. For B06 (after 0.8 s) to B07 it lies face up in her lap, because the script has her lower it to her lap while it keeps recording, and her right hand is busy with the sunglasses and his chin in B07.
- White-apartment compass words are rotated 90° to match LOC-07, where PR-39 faces 270° and she walks east into the flat. LOC-08 and LOC-10 now read A west, B north, C east, D south, and no letter or coordinate moved. The note asked for B south and D north. That would mirror the plan instead of rotating it: the walls would run counter-clockwise, against the craft rule, and the bathroom on wall B would sit on his right, against 'right on the left'. So LOC-08's window (wall C) faces east and takes the morning sun, and LOC-10's only window (wall D) faces south, behind a white roller blind. Old 'north end' references in LOC-08 now read 'east end'. LOC-09 gains its own compass: entered walking north.
- SC18 tabletop, following the note: from B12 the printer (PR-25) is at x 145, y 340, on Venus's side, next to the person who works it. The oranges (PR-24) are at x 195-225, y 340-360, in front of Jean's chair, so 'lantern and oranges in front of him' holds on plan. The sunglasses (PR-04) go at x 200-214, y 365-370, just north of the oranges.
- PR-02 gains an SC18 state. She cannot sit close on a spindle chair with a pistol grip-up in her back pocket, so before she sits she lays it on its side on PR-17's south-west corner (x 124-136, y 331-348), muzzle toward the door, by her right hand and away from the chairs' shared edge. The DP confirms it stays out of the front-camera photo.
- PR-16's foil wrap is deleted, overruling the earlier film-director and commissioner notes, because whether her face reads in V01-B12 is a framing and lighting call for the camera department. The lantern is a practical, unwrapped, with all-round output.
- PR-52's ironed fold grid is dropped because it contradicts SC17's 'no crease left'. The fresh set reads as new by being flat, even, pressed and a shade whiter (#FBFAF7) than the quilted mattress (#F2F0EA), against S1's rumpled set with #E4E1DA fold shadow. No writer change is needed.
- PR-20 is all landscape on a fixed 17.5 x 12 cm grid (2.5 cm and 2 cm gaps, ±1 cm hand-pinned), keeping 180 prints on wall C and 70 on wall B, 250 in all, so two runs build the same wall. The only portrait-format prints in the room are PR-28 and PR-27, added in SC18. About 250 is an ASSUMED count that meets the verified fact that the walls are covered in photos of him, although the script says 'dozens'.
- PR-53's stated size and footprint now match: 50 x 40 cm at x 60-110, y 365-405, with PR-01 and PR-54 still at y 385.
- The wet ring is re-wet rather than redressed as dry. PR-33's SC05 ring dries by the SC08 montage. Jean's fresh, sweating coupe (PR-32 S3) rocks on the base straw and leaves a new ring, so S03's 'one wall straw in a wet ring' holds. The SC08 beat has been passed to the writer.
- SC11 off-screen set, while Jean is in the bathroom: the jacket goes onto PR-62, PR-03 onto PR-42 at x 175, y 535, PR-30 into her tumbler's ice, and PR-01 onto PR-53. That is how the tin and the straw reach SC12 after she changes into a T-shirt with no pockets.
- The SC17 exterior insert is set at LOC-07's street frontage. Off screen before it, she fetched the car from outside the bar and moved PR-08 and PR-09 into PR-06, so the passenger seat is empty in the insert and throughout V01.
- Motel room finish: knotty-pine tongue-and-groove paneling, the period roadside-motel wall. It takes the pins, and the paler rectangle on wall C is where the motel's own picture kept the pine from ambering.
- Car: silver-grey paint with bone leather, so the REC dot stays the only red in the drive. The red car in ref 11 is rejected for that reason. The collar-on-headrest contrast is corrected to 6.8:1 (#111111 on #D9CBB0); the previous 11.8:1 was a calculation error. Every other contrast value was rechecked and stands.
- Straws are plain white paper, so the house reads on the dark counter at 12:1. The tin is satin gunmetal rather than bright steel, because bright steel on white gloss falls below 3:1.
- The car heads north on every filmed pass in SC01, so the low west sun sits behind Venus and Jean's turned face is in shade. The loop east and south happens off screen between B02 and B03.
- In LOC-04 the chairs stand side by side with their backs to the photo wall, facing the door. V01-B13 then reads as a table set for two, and the SC18 front-camera photo has Jean's wall behind both of them.
- Dust is owned by the set and tracked as states under PR-57: the dash, Jean's collar (agreed with the stylist) and his eyes (handed to makeup for V01-B07).
- Legible text, written verbatim for compositing: the phone UI timers as scripted (V01 00:00:04 to 01:04:55; V02 00:06:13 to 01:05:00); 'STOP'; 'MOTEL'; 'OFFICE'; 'NO FURTHER ACTION'; and the dash-cam time '01:47:12' (ASSUMED). Nothing else is legible.
- Surfaces that must stay blank, because generators add fake logos: the car's grille, wings, wheels, dials, plate and keys; the storefronts; the bottle labels, fridge, chalkboard, neon and coasters; the rideshare's badges and stickers; the dash cam; the mailboxes, door and facade; the speaker, ice bucket and pedal bin; both phones and PR-01's side button; the organiser, vial and toilet paper; the cans, jugs and printer; Jean's duffel and Venus's holdall; the gas pump and courthouse in the prints; and the T-shirt (stylist).
- There is no product (route R2 stretch). Each product_placement field names the device doing the job, its contrast and its clear zone instead, and the taste note 'the product is a tool, then a hero' does not apply.
- Taste notes: each location names its one real detail. Broken on purpose: the white apartment has no dish rack or charging cable, because its tidiness is the horror, and ref 06's patterned bedding is replaced with white to hold the colour theme.
- The craft limit of about five locked props per frame is exceeded in V01-B13 and S02 because the script names more; LOC-04 gives a lock order.
- References 01-11 were not inspected, since this run has no reference reader. They are used only as the brief describes them, and no character or set is modelled on a real person or a real, recognisable place.
- Camera, lenses and lighting belong to the DP. The only requests made here: the stylist's light-wash denim for the pistol grip; the tin's mirror path; the practical lantern, unwrapped, with all-round output; the dust hand-offs to the stylist and makeup; the locked car section sent to the cinematographer; and a framing check that PR-02 stays out of the SC18 photo.
- Blocker (Writer (copywriter)): The re-wet ring (PR-32 S3) needs a beat the script doesn't have. In the SC08 window montage, Jean's fresh coupe has to be set down on the base straw, rock, and be lifted off, so that S03's 'one wall straw in a wet ring' is physically true hours after SC05. → Default used here: the beat plays silently through the window and is cut on his laugh. Please add one line to SC08, for example 'He sets his coupe down on the base straw; it rocks; he lifts it off, laughing.' No change is needed to the S03 stills copy.
- Blocker (Writer (copywriter)): In SC11 Jean wipes the rim and sees faint pink on the tissue, but dry blood on glazed china flakes off brown. The pink needs a damp tissue, which the script doesn't give him. → Default used here: he wets the tissue under the basin tap before wiping (PR-48 S2). Please confirm, or cut the pink.
- Blocker (Production Designer (car reference model) and the cinematographer): The car's section is now locked for its reference model (A09). Cushion tops 35 cm, dash top 88 cm, windscreen rail 118 cm with no mirror, headrest pad tops 108 cm. Eye lines: Venus 110 cm (crown about 121 cm), Jean 103 cm slumped. Top-up outside height 1.30 m, 6 cm over her crown. The camera plan still carries the old values (rail 110 cm, pads 122 cm, eye lines 125 and 118 cm). → Build the car's generation reference model to these values and send the section to the cinematographer to re-check eye line against rail and pads for SC01 and the top-up SC04. These are locked design values, not measurements from cast or doubles, and any change comes back to production design. The cushion-raise fallback is withdrawn.
- Blocker (Producer (A10)): Clearance is pending for the original phone UI and for the 'Naked and Famous' cocktail name, and a 1950s roadster silhouette risks reading as a real make's trade dress. → Everything is built unbranded, with a generic grille and filled badge holes. If the UI reads as a real OS, simplify it to the REC dot and timer, as the route allows. Nothing is claimed as cleared.

### Director's treatment

- Lucas's request (camera department at full 10s, 35mm film style) is honoured as the script decided. Outside the phone is 35mm film; inside the phone keeps its compression and UI. The texture switches at the frame open and close (V01-B06, B08; SC04's trade). Stock, grain, lenses and grade belong to the camera department. My staging supports the look: full-width setups are locked off, everything plays at real speed, and D01 action is practical.
- Dead-state rule, replacing rigor as the explanation everywhere: Jean's head moves only when her hand moves it, stays where she leaves it, and never lolls or bobs. No medical cause is stated on screen or in this treatment.
- Bearings. In LOC-02, LOC-03 and LOC-04 I use compass bearings, north 0°. After B07, with the car heading north, Jean's face points at bearing about 30° (60° off the door), matching the camera plan's SH15. Seated facing south in SC18, the same set is 60° to his own right, so his face points at bearing about 240°. LOC-01 and LOC-08 coordinates follow the world bible.
- SC18: his eyes miss the lens because she holds the phone above his level gaze and never lowers it. Her chin push is upward and doesn't move him (the script's 'it doesn't give').
- D01 scenes SC04-SC18 are staged for a live-action pilot shoot. The 5 s rule and the no-hand-off rule apply to the generated V01, V02 and stills; the hand-offs in SC08 and SC12 are practical only.
- Venus holds the phone in her right hand and drives with her left. Her right thumb carries the nick (casting), and I keep the proposed left-index burn; it reads on the B02 collar hand and the SC18 chin push.
- Wind is my choice: relative, nose to tail, in the moving car; from the highway (south) at the motel, which also explains the wind at room 3's south-facing door.
- B03's storefront cut is the loop's second northbound pass (world bible LOC-02).
- SC03: because PR-25 sits at x 145, Venus works from the table's south edge rather than its east end, and the chair drag is cut into the action at 78.5.
- The SC11 off-screen set is done by Venus alone while Jean is in the bathroom; it is heard only as ice.
- Phone and printer pair automatically in SC18, so there is no on-screen tapping to print.
- D01 timings cover the key physical actions; dialogue and holds fill the rest of each scene's slot.
- There is no product, so claim_demo_needs_clearance doesn't arise. The verified story facts are staged exactly, each with an acceptance test: the wall with none of them together, the gun in her back pocket at the motel, the meds untaken for weeks, the dried blood behind the toilet seat, the trance-soul music, Jean in black, and the white colour theme.
- Taste notes broken on purpose: S01 shows her laughing face, because a poster needs its lead; the B04 and B08 fallbacks show part of her face for under a second, as the route accepts; LOC-08 has no dish rack or cable, because the tidiness is the horror. All other notes are followed: hands before faces, sound design before music, restraint over explanation, no slow motion.
- No reference image was inspected; refs 05 and 06 are used only as the brief describes them.
- Blocker (production designer): The world bible's LOC-01 pose P2 sets Jean's face at car-frame bearing 270° (dead ahead). This treatment and the camera plan's SH15 set it 60° off the door, which is 300° in the car frame (compass about 30° heading north), and SC18 depends on that set. → Change P2 to car-frame 300° (compass about 30° with the car heading north) and add: 'seated facing south in SC18, 60° to his own right, bearing about 240°'.
- Blocker (writer; casting director): Rigor is still given as the explanation in the script's crew annex ('his head stays where she set it (rigor)' for SC01; 'His chin doesn't give (rigor)' for SC18) and in the head-set decision ('the rigor logic in the casting bible'). The casting bible's body rule ('neck fixed from the first frame of the drive') implies the same timeline, which the SC17 dressing contradicts. → Writer: replace each rigor reference with 'his head moves only when her hand moves it, stays where she leaves it, and never lolls or bobs'. SC18's reason becomes 'she holds the phone above his level gaze and doesn't lower it'. Casting: restate the body rule as this performance rule, and keep 'his arms still bend' only as a note for the practical dressing.
- Blocker (production designer): SC18 has no source for the three pins: two for PR-28 and one for PR-27. → Add to PR-28 S1: the next man's prints come out of PR-06 with pins already through their top edges, plus one spare pin in the top print for PR-27.
- Blocker (cinematographer): V02-B04 to B06 need about 7 s of phone-frame footage on Jean's open eyes, but V01-B08's phone section runs only 4.0 s (52.0-56.0). → Generate the B08 eyes plate with handles (two plates of up to 5 s joined on a laugh jolt) at 1080x1920 or better, and shoot the thumb composite plate long.
- Blocker (writer): SC11 carries plot (A13, the previous man's drops) but has no honest link to the route idea; nothing on screen ties the drops to a photograph. It is staged as written, and its link field says so. → Writer's call: accept SC11 as a plot scene while SC18 carries the idea. Or add a 2 s beat before he lifts the seat: Jean takes a mirror selfie in PR-49 with PR-35, one more photo of him alone in her home, with no her in it.

### Looks

- Season and temperature are assumed: early October 2026 in the US Southwest (the world bible gives only 'US, 2026 ASSUMED'). That puts the city at night at about 19 °C, the apartment at 21 °C, the desert drive at 27 °C falling to 17 °C, and the motel at night at about 15 °C.
- US sizing follows the casting bible. Skin depth and undertone come from its cheekbone hexes. Heritage is recorded there and never prompted, so every look restates depth, undertone and curl pattern instead.
- Lucas's 35mm request is for the camera department; wardrobe only makes sure the looks hold on it. Every garment is a solid with no fine stripe, check or small rib that would boil under grain. Whites sit at L* 90-94 and blacks at L* 15-16, each with its fabric's cast, so both keep texture. The film look never lightens either lead's skin (casting joint lock).
- Jean's black: the route and set palettes use #111111, which I treat as the graded read. In a neutral grade the fabric is blue-black #24262B (approx L*15), per the craft rule that blacks sit near L* 15-22.
- The VENUS_L01 review note is applied as written: a right chest welt with a 9 cm opening and a 13.5 cm bag, leaving 1.5 cm of glass above the welt. PR-01's weight isn't given, so I assume about 200 g; stay tapes to the shoulder carry it, which keeps the front plumb. Riding lens end down keeps the rear lens and its smear inside the pocket, so only bare black shows. Production design confirms this in PR-01 S2.
- The VENUS_L03 review note is applied: the front pocket bags are 19 cm deep (over the 18 cm asked) and stayed into the fly. The phone's top sits at the waistband's lower seam, under the 3 cm belt. The pistol (right back), phone (right front) and keys (left front, per PR-61) sit in three separate pockets. Only the grip shows, plus the flat phone outline PR-01 S7 asks for.
- The JEAN_L01 review note is applied, and it reverses my last version. The world bible (PR-04 S0, PR-56) currently keeps the sunglasses in the duffel pocket because I had ruled out the placket; I now rule it in. Seen from SC04, the glasses are planted before V01 needs them, and they suit a man who buys clothes to be looked at. Later she wears them the same way, hooked into her own neckline (V01-B08), with nothing said (taste note: restraint over explanation). Production design updates PR-04, PR-05 and PR-56 (see blockers).
- The casting bible now puts both the scar and the thumb nick on her right hand, gives the collar touch to her left, and proposes a left-index burn. My old hands blocker is withdrawn. Both sleeves still end 12 cm above the wrist bone, so any of the three marks reads.
- Jean's hair follows the updated casting anchor: the top is 12-15 cm stretched and picked out at the crown, replacing my earlier 8-10 cm.
- Venus's jacket stays waist length, with the hem 1 cm above the waistband. The 6 cm grip clears it in SC04, and no sit fold crosses either pocket.
- Venus changes looks only when the story does: the bar (L01), the tee at home (L02, changed off screen in SC11), then the shirt for the drive (L03, from SC17). The bone jacket comes back at the end of SC17 to take the tin and the flat straw, then goes folded into the boot, unseen, through V01.
- The nape puff for the open car is the stylist's call, per the casting bible. It keeps hair off the hand shots and avoids the slow-motion hair the brief bans.
- There is no product (R2 stretch), so I ran the product hue rule against the story's two reds instead: the REC dot #D7261E and the dried drops #6E1C18. Every garment worn in frame with them is under C* 15 or far off in hue. Skin and hair are excluded, because skin is never altered.
- Every look has one garment older than the story, with a rebuild method: Venus's pistol-worn back pocket and her L02 tee, Jean's belt, the bartender's apron, and the driver's sun-faded shirt (taste note: one real detail per scene).
- Taste notes followed: hands before faces (every acting hand is bare, with cuffs at mid-forearm or above) and restraint over explanation (no symbolic jewellery; her whites and his black carry the verified colour theme). One craft rule is broken on purpose: VENUS_L02 stays inside the white room's palette so that Jean is the only dark mass, because the tone makes the room's tidiness the horror.
- No reference image was inspected, since no reference_reader is bound. The references are used only as the brief describes them, and no character is modelled on a face in them.
- Blocker (Production designer (copywriter optional)): This version puts Jean's sunglasses in his shirt placket, which contradicts the world bible. PR-04 S0 keeps them in PR-05's outside pocket from SC04 to SC16, PR-05 S5 takes them out in SC17, and PR-56 says nothing hangs from the placket in SC04-SC15. All three were written against my previous line, which this version reverses on the commissioner's note. → Change PR-04 to: SC04-SC14, hooked by its left arm in Jean's placket over the fastened second button, held by a clear thread tack; SC15, laid folded on top of the folded shirt on PR-55 (PR-56 S2); S1 in SC17, taken from that pile and put on his face. Remove PR-04 from PR-05's outside pocket and delete PR-56's placket note. If the writer wants it on the page, SC15 can add 'she lays his sunglasses on top'.
- Blocker (Cinematographer / colourist and production designer): Jean's black is #111111 in the route and set palettes (PR-04, PR-56, PR-57 C2), and PR-41 is still #151515. The costume is specified as blue-black #24262B fabric and brown-black #2A2624 leather, per the craft rule for blacks at L* 15-22. LOC-01 now gives #111111 on the #D9CBB0 headrest as 6.8:1. By WCAG relative luminance that pair computes to about 11.8:1, and the neutral-grade fabric #24262B to about 9.5:1. → The colourist grades Jean toward the route's #111111 read within the 35mm look. Production design sets PR-41 to #2A2624, notes the SH-1 to SH-4 and TR-1/TR-2 builds against PR-56's states, and rechecks the LOC-01 ratio. Both values clear with room to spare.
- Blocker (Production designer and film director): Two PR-01 pocket details need sign-off from the departments that own them. (a) The phone rides lens end down in the jacket's right chest welt, so only bare black glass shows above it; PR-01 S2 currently says only 'screen-in'. (b) In SC18 the phone is in her right front jeans pocket, and it would bend across the hip crease if she sits before taking it out. → Production design adds 'lens end down' to PR-01 S2. The director blocks SC18 so that she takes the phone out standing, as she sets PR-02 on the table (PR-02 S4), and then sits.
- Blocker (Lucas (creative lead) via producer, with production designer): Lucas's 'oversized Raptors t shirt' loses its team. The L02 tee reads as an athletic shirt by its raglan cut and V-notch, but no team, city or colour can be shown until A10 clears the real shirt or a fictional team is designed and cleared. → Lucas chooses: accept the unmarked athletic tee, or have production design create a fictional team for the producer to clear under A10. If team colours come in, wardrobe re-runs the white-room rule break, because a saturated or dark team colour would make a second dark mass beside Jean.
- Blocker (Lucas (creative lead) via casting director): Lucas still has to confirm heritage for VENUS and JEAN (carried over from the casting bible). Hair and makeup here are written in skin depth, undertone and curl pattern only. → If casting changes, only the skin hexes, curl patterns and grooming lines in VENUS_L01-L03 and JEAN_L01-L02 need updating. The garments stand.

### Sound

- Timing is in seconds from each deliverable's first frame at 24 fps, with frame = round(s x 24). V01 hits are re-spotted to the camera plan's global frames as the three package reviews relay them (marked CP in SND-V01-017). The camera plan itself was not in this prompt. Hits it doesn't give are targets (T), to be spotted to the generated picture, picture-led at ±2.
- The cut into B03 at f240 comes from the script's beat boundary. The camera plan frames relayed are f302 and f367, so f240 is to be confirmed against it.
- The side-button click stays on f689, which all three notes give. The film director's 'two frames before the LED at f696' doesn't match f689 to f696, which is 7 frames. The light itself stays silent (restraint over explanation).
- The SH12/SH13 cut and SH13's torso-shake frames were not relayed. The laugh is recorded while the actor watches the generated SH13 picture, so its peaks can land within 2 frames.
- The camera plan's room order (oranges, jugs, printer, chair) has no can action. The script's 'cans set down on wood' is served by the sack set down in the dark before the lantern (f1730-f1742, sound-led).
- The door shoves in SH22 and the padlock tugs have no relayed frames. Their targets hold the required order (sticks, then scrapes; steps stop, then the tugs) and move to the shoulder and hand contacts in the generated picture.
- V02 in-points: SH01 from V01 5.5 s, SH02 from 15.3 s, SH03 from 42.8-43.8 s, and SH04 from V01 f1080, pending the cinematographer's lock. B04-B06 follow the SH17 plate plan: plate A f144-f264 (3.0 s of eyes, then a 2.0 s laugh), plate B the thumb, joined at f264 on a laugh jolt. The earlier 7.0 s continuous-take premise and its blocker are dropped.
- The mic cut is at V02 f312, the first frame of SH08, as all notes agree. The commissioner's 'SH07 f43' equals f312 if SH07 starts at the thumb onset (f269); if SH07 starts at the plate join (f264), the same cut is SH07 f48.
- The 35mm film style is a picture treatment. Sound carries the switch from phone to film at f835 by changing mic perspective (clipped phone mic to a clean exterior), and adds no projector, gate or optical-track sound.
- V01 and V02 have no spoken lines (A15). Venus's laugh is the only voice, and Jean makes no sound. Native clip audio is muted, and no generated audio is used.
- V02-B01's 'wind on the mic' is played as a light breeze from the stop-sign session, because the driving wind is gone at the stop.
- V02-B02's REC 00:52:30 shot is V01's third jump cut (f367). V01 calls it scrub hills and V02 calls it open desert; the sound follows the shot.
- The picture car is generated, and its sound is recorded on a hired vintage open two-seater. That such a car can be hired is a hypothesis, and no make is identified.
- The D01 cues are pilot anchors, not full coverage. Their times come from the outline's scene times and move with the pilot edit.
- SC17's 4 s kerb insert plays in silence because the script calls it 'Four silent seconds'. Room tone returns on the cut upstairs.
- The PR-54 volume ring sound (wool rub over a soft detent) follows the world bible's description as relayed in the review notes. It is recorded on the prop.
- 100 BPM, the four-bar loop and the bar-4 hole are a proposal for the commission, not a spec from Lucas.
- The tin has a hinged lid (a prop choice for production design to confirm). Nothing inside it is ever heard.
- Loudness and delivery specs are left to the mix.
- The Venus, Jean and Bartender actors are not yet cast, so every voice consent stays pending.
- Gun-handling foley uses a non-firing replica, for production safety to confirm.
- The 2-frame dropout at the front-camera flip is a design choice, not a claim about how any phone behaves.
- Having no laugh in the tag is a proposal awaiting approval.
- Taste notes followed: sound design before music (no score in V01 or V02, and the pilot's only music plays inside the scene); restraint over explanation (no stings on the eyes, gun, tin, light or title); hands before faces (hand foley leads each beat); dry over sentimental. None is broken on purpose.
